## lc-15
grade: DONE
requirement: An item whose `blocked-by` names another item by id is validated against nothing. A blocker of the declared form `<prefix>-<n>` pointing at an id the carrier does not contain passes `item check` CLEAN and `kind check` CLEAN — measured, by accident, with a real mistake: lc-14 was written `blocked-by: lc-15` when no lc-15 existed, and both checkers reported clean. The consequence is a PERMANENT SILENT PARK: the item never surfaces in `item ready` because it reads as blocked, and nothing ever reports that the blocker is fictional, so it can neither drain nor be noticed.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: red-first against a carrier carrying a blocker id that does not resolve: a named finding, green once the id resolves or the blocker is retyped. The three other blocker forms (`decision <q>`, `evidence <predicate>`, NONE) must NOT fire — they resolve against nothing by design, and a check that cannot tell them apart from a dangling id would fire on legitimate work.
evidence: executed: `item check` -> "CLEAN — 0 shape finding(s)", `kind check` -> "CLEAN — 19 kind(s) registered", both with the dangling id in place. REF_TYPES (declaration.py:103) is ("lane","verb","hook","session","producer","operator") — DECLARATION reference types; an item-carrier id is a different namespace and appears in none of them. DISTINCT from the refusal table's recorded `route_set_unwatched`, which is about the declaration resolver narrowed to `lane:`; this is the ITEM carrier's own blocker slot.

## lc-44
grade: DONE
requirement: item close accepts --reason on a DONE close and writes it NOWHERE — not into the moved body, not into the ledger, not into the commit message body. Measured 2026-08-27 in dotfiles: df-143 closed with a 900-char reason naming its commit ref and verification basis; grep for that ref afterwards returns 0 in ITEMS.md, 0 in ITEMS-DONE.md, 0 in LEDGER.md. Reading confirms it: in cmd_item_close, reason is bound once and consumed only inside the 'if args.drop:' branch (ledger dropped: line). The silent direction is the whole defect — the caller sees 'moved df-N to ITEMS-DONE.md (grade DONE)' plus a commit and reads that as a complete closure record.
goal: one-home-per-kind
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py (cmd_item_close), and the close verb's --help text
done-criterion: a DONE close persists its reason where the doctrine says closures live — items leave BY COMMIT REF, and today the ref cannot survive the verb that closes the item. Red-first: close an item with a reason naming a ref, grep all three carriers for that ref, expect 0 on the old arm and non-zero on the new. Must-not-move: a --drop close still writes exactly one ledger dropped: line and no second copy; an omitted --reason on a DONE close behaves as today. Decide and state which home a DONE reason takes — the moved body or a ledger closed: line — and refuse the OTHER, because two homes for one fact is the paraphrase-drift the carrier doctrine forbids.
evidence: dotfiles LEDGER.md 2026-08-27 carries df-143's closure record written BY HAND with a note saying why; dotfiles df-1 lost its entire closure basis the same way, and the desk reported that basis to the judgment desk as persisted when it was not.
blocked-by: NONE

amended-done-criterion: 2026-08-27 RULED 2026-08-27 (judgment desk): a DONE reason lives in the MOVED BODY, never the ledger — two lines, 'closed-reason: <date> <text>' and 'closed-ref: <sha>', following the promote precedent (no slot separator inside a value), written in the SAME buffer write as the move. A DROP keeps its ledger line, because a dropped body may be pruned and its record cannot live only there; the ledger stays decisions, supersessions and drops. Red-first: close an item with a reason naming a ref, grep all three carriers for that ref — 0 on the old arm, non-zero in ITEMS-DONE.md on the new. Must-not-move: a --drop close still writes exactly one ledger dropped: line and no second copy; an omitted --reason on a DONE close behaves as today; no closed-reason/closed-ref line appears on a DROP. DISCHARGE IT ALSO CARRIES: the first DONE closure landing a closed-ref after this is the real-repo proof of the ITEMS-carrier accept half of df-143's guard fix, which C1 could only exercise in scratch fixtures.
amend-reason: 2026-08-27 the booked write-set named verbs.py and the --help text only; the slot REGISTRATION in items.py was required and landed in cbfaee6, and the ref refusal needs a registry row in refusals.py or the emit-site coverage check fails

grade: DONE
requirement: migrate writes decision-blocker QUESTIONS containing the ledgers own slot separator, so the blocker can never be answered: ledger add decision REFUSES the question (FINDING ledger_body, correctly — an escaped spelling would put two forms of every value in the file), while lc-26 resolves a decision blocker by QUESTION-SLOT EQUALITY. The two mechanisms are individually right and jointly make the item permanently blocked

evidence: measured by the wave-4 desk 2026-08-27 over dotfiles ITEMS.md: 99 decision-blocked items, 69 of them carry " — " in the question. blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-08-27 DECIDED at the wave-4 peer desk 2026-08-27: option ONE, migrate sanitises at mint time. The other two are refused on the carrier doctrine, not on cost. Escaping in the ledger puts two spellings of every value in the file and the reader cannot tell which it is looking at, which lc-40 own requirement already says. Matching on a normalised form is a comparison over transformed text standing in for the parsed body, so the stored question and the matched question would differ by construction, and that is the paraphrase-drift the doctrine forbids. The minter is the ONE place that controls the text: a question that cannot be stored is a question that must never be minted, so the fix belongs where it is written, not where it is read. SEPARATELY DECIDED: the 69 already-written blockers ARE repaired in place by a batched item amend, using the fixed minter exact output, at this desk after the code lands; B2 pass 2 waits on that pass. NOTE the irony and it is evidence not decoration: this decision could not be recorded through ledger add decision, because the blocker question itself carries the separator and the ledger correctly refuses it. The defect bit its own item on the way to being fixed
amended-blocked-by: 2026-08-27 NONE
closed-reason: 2026-08-28 the defect is fixed and the criterion that outlived it is superseded here. lc-40 asked that a minted blocker question be storable VERBATIM; that is unsatisfiable by design, since the ledger refuses separator-bearing text and the same design refuses both escaping and normalisation. The satisfiable property is STORABLE: the minter never produces a question the ledger would refuse. Option ONE landed at c5164f7 (migrate sanitises at mint time) and a146b62 put the minting form beside the predicate that judges it, in the shared grammar module. Verified at this desk 2026-08-28: c5164f7 is an ancestor of HEAD, and the ledger refusal still fires at HEAD on a separator-bearing question with the tree untouched by the refused call

## lc-19
grade: DONE
requirement: AMENDED 2026-08-26 — the original diagnosis (a _GRADE_WORD anchoring defect) was WRONG and is replaced; the measurement stands. The real cause: UNCLASSIFIED is a MISSING RULE, not a missing match. classify() matches the grade word and then does RULES.get(word); a word with no rule yields grade=None → UNCLASSIFIED. The RULES key set is BUST, CANDIDATE, FINDING, HANDOFF, NEW, OPEN, PARKED, PARTLY, POINTER, READY, RECORD — there is NO DONE and NO DROPPED. So every properly-graded closure in a source carrier is unclassified by construction. DROPPED is the sharp one: it belongs to the plugin's OWN default grade vocabulary (READY/PARKED/DONE/DROPPED) and still has no rule. Same root cause as lc-18 — the tool expects closures to arrive via --from-done, so the in-carrier closure vocabulary was never given rules
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,test/test_migrate.py
done-criterion: the closure vocabulary classifies rather than falling through — DONE and DROPPED at minimum, plus a declared way for a repo to map its own closure words; red-first on a fixture carrying DONE and DROPPED entries, asserting they do NOT land in the open carrier. A word with no rule must still be reported as unclassified rather than guessed at — the fix is rules, never a looser matcher
evidence: verified here at 40b9c36 by executing the module: sorted(RULES.keys()) returns the 11 words above, 'DONE' in RULES is False, 'DROPPED' in RULES is False. Counts are the peer's executed measurement over files this session did not open, CORRECTED by them post-booking: root BACKLOG.md UNCLASSIFIED 83 = DONE 76 + DROPPED 5 + ERLEDIGT 1 + RESOLVED 1; claude/BACKLOG.md 18 of 66 = DONE 14 + DROPPED 2 + TRACED 1 + EXECUTED 1 (66/18 post-dates their e3b3ebf, which added one Done entry; use these, not the earlier 65/17)

## lc-20
grade: DROPPED
requirement: `lifecycle init`'s laws-file branch keys on the git AUTHOR HISTORY of CLAUDE.md, so it answers differently in any mirror, worktree or fresh clone than in the origin — and the wrong answers are plausible enough to book. The deciding rule has three branches (own repo -> CLAUDE.md; foreign tracked CLAUDE.md -> the local overlay; absent -> could-not-verify), and which one fires depends on state the operator does not think of as input: whether the file is tracked at all, and who authored the commits. Nothing in the output announces that the answer is arrangement-dependent.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/init.py,test/test_init.py

evidence: peer measurement (dotfiles desk, 2026-08-26), three runs of `lifecycle init` in a scratch mirror of dotfiles giving three different `laws` readings: (1) 'no tracked CLAUDE.md -> overlay branch', the mirror's tree untracked because a `git add -q` had silently failed; (2) 'foreign branch', the mirror's commit authored x@y; (3) the correct 'operator-only branch' once committed as the operator's own address. NOT a test defect: lifecycle's own test_init.py pins the fixture author deliberately, with a persisted user.email/user.name for what determine_laws reads and a per-commit -c override for authoring history as someone else — verified here at cf92ad9. This is the verb in the field.

The criterion admitted only deleted-or-bannered; the lane measured a third terminal state the design forces: under --merge, bannering shifts lines that older unpinned anchors cite, so those citations would still RESOLVE while pointing at the wrong content - the 313-of-318 defect this family has already measured elsewhere. Standing down and saying so is the only branch that neither creates silently-wrong citations nor blocks a legitimate merge, and its remedy is lc-96 (the re-rooting verb), after which the freeze proceeds unchanged. Left as written, the entry would have read UNSATISFIED against a correct build and the next lane would have chased it
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-13 a fresh migration leaves no live-reading superseded carrier, by one of THREE named dispositions - the run says which, and silence is not one of them: DELETED (source unlinked, deletion record in the declared laws file, citations carrying inline blob pins), FROZEN (the default for a writing run: a banner in the source's head, pins on every anchor this run writes), or UNTOUCHED. UNTOUCHED covers two cases and both are deliberate: a run that writes no successor state at all, and - added 2026-09-13 - a --merge whose freeze would MOVE a line that a surviving unpinned anchor already cites, where the disposition stands down and reports the reason with its count. ## lc-89
grade: DONE
requirement: The pre-push leak scan prints 'fatal: Not a valid object name ^{commit}' on every push, then 'degraded: base ref  is not resolvable here - scanning everything at HEAD' with an empty ref name. Cause read at the source: when stdin yields no parseable ranges the hook falls back to the literal range '..HEAD' (tools/git-hooks/pre-push, the ranges_from_stdin fallback), whose EMPTY base the scanner hands to git rev-parse. The fallback is deliberate and its coverage is correct - scanning everything beats a clean run over zero bytes - so this is NOT an open door. What it costs is the reader: a fatal line on every ordinary push is a check firing on a non-defect, which trains the discount reflex that kills a guard the day its red is real
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: tools/git-hooks/pre-push,test/
done-criterion: an ordinary push prints no 'fatal:' line and no empty ref name: the no-ranges fallback states in its own words that it is scanning the whole of HEAD by design, rather than reaching git with an empty base and reporting the failure as degradation. Red-first is in hand and must be reproduced: a push at this HEAD prints both lines. MUST-NOT-MOVE, and it is the whole risk: the fallback still scans EVERYTHING reachable from HEAD, and a genuinely unresolvable base still degrades loudly rather than silently - the repair is to the message and the empty ref, never to the coverage
evidence: OBSERVED at this desk 2026-09-12 in this session own push output (d87a4af..bf38401), then read at the source: tools/git-hooks/pre-push builds ranges=['..HEAD'] when ranges_from_stdin returns empty, with the comment 'Scan everything reachable from HEAD rather than declaring a clean run over zero bytes'. The scanner side is tools/absence-scan.mjs rangeCommitArgs. Not relayed
blocked-by: NONE

Two further instances landed 2026-09-13 - one observed here, one relayed from the judgment desk - and they settle that question: desk-independent, ordinary single-commit push. Recorded now rather than when the wave reaches the entry, because this desk's context was cleared once today and a fact held only in chat does not survive the next one. Also separates a second, unrelated pre-push line (the unbooked-commit mark warning) that appeared alongside and is NOT in this entry's scope
amended-evidence: 2026-09-13 OBSERVED at this desk 2026-09-12 in this session own push output (d87a4af..bf38401), then read at the source: tools/git-hooks/pre-push builds ranges=['..HEAD'] when ranges_from_stdin returns empty, with the comment 'Scan everything reachable from HEAD rather than declaring a clean run over zero bytes'. The scanner side is tools/absence-scan.mjs rangeCommitArgs. Not relayed. REPRODUCED 2026-09-13, twice, on ordinary single-commit pushes of this repo at two different desks: (i) at this desk pushing ebb0133..20bf33a, output verbatim 'fatal: Not a valid object name ^{commit}' then 'degraded: base ref  is not resolvable here - scanning everything at HEAD' with the empty ref name, push succeeded and absence-scan reported clean - OBSERVED here; (ii) at the judgment desk dotfiles-9e pushing ebb0133, the same two lines, RELAYED by that desk from its own transcript and not re-read here. So the noise is desk-independent and fires on the ordinary case, not on anything about one checkout. One difference to separate when the fix is scoped: this desk's push ALSO printed pre-push '1 unbooked commit(s) WITHOUT A MARK' over a commit that carries a Claude-Session trailer, which the judgment desk did not report seeing - that line is a different mechanism (the subagent-commit-mark recorder), not the empty-base fallback, and lc-89's criterion does not cover it
closed-reason: 2026-09-13 Landed by a dispatched opus lane under the guard-checker-bau devbook, plus one desk commit, and the item found four things larger than itself. THE FIX: the no-ranges fallback handed the scanner an empty base where the scanner's sentinel is the literal token EMPTY, so every ordinary push printed 'fatal: Not a valid object name ^{commit}' and a degraded line naming an empty ref. Coverage was never wrong; the reader was, and a check firing on a non-defect trains the reflex that discounts a real red. Both sites are repaired - the fallback (0cbd1ad) and the new-ref first push (7b5aafe) - with the docstring that produced BOTH corrected: it said the contract 'accepts an EMPTY old', which reads as English 'accepts a blank one'. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK: 368 tests OK 0 skipped, lifecycle --test CLEAN 77/77, mode 100755 in HEAD, and the gate git actually runs executes quiet and exits 0. S2 SETTLED BY EXECUTION rather than by my reasoning: my brief claimed the two spellings might scan different sets and told the lane to HALT if they did. They cannot - scanGitRange assigns from='EMPTY' in its degrade branch before any walk begins and oldRef is used nowhere else - so the halt condition I wrote could never fire. The lane proved it with GIT_TRACE over two fixtures plus a positive control on a known-different pair. FOUR FINDINGS, none of them the item. (1) A FAIL-OPEN GATE, created and repaired inside the lane: 0cbd1ad committed the hook at mode 100644 with the same blob sha, because a Python file write drops the mode while every prescribed restore check - sha256 equal, git status clean - passes. The leak gate was dead until d8c3934. Booked as lc-103; the devbook's mode-blind restore step is routed to the judgment desk. (2) THE SCANNER'S FALLBACK SCANS NO RANGE INTERIOR while its message says it scans everything: measured here against real history, rev-list returns 0 commits while ls-tree returns 57 files, so a blob added and removed inside a push is never seen and the run reads clean. Booked as lc-102, cross-repo. (3) A CONTROL THAT ASSERTED THE PROBED PROPERTY went red for the defect and certified nothing - the lane caught it in its own battery and left the reasoning in the file. (4) A LIVE CO-WRITER: this desk granted G2 to the lane three times, each grant sat behind the lane's report composition, and when it idled a third time the desk took the work itself WITHOUT withdrawing the grant. Both parties held the same job. The lane's patch caught it because its anchor assertion failed and it LOOKED rather than re-anchoring; it also declined to run prove-rows, which would have restored the desk's uncommitted edits away silently. Nothing was lost. The double assignment was the desk's error, recorded here because the carrier is where it will be read.

The data cannot carry the fix, so the verb must

The reported mechanism was WRONG and the lane measured it: move_to_done already cleared the blocked-by LINE, so a base-slot blocker never reached the closure home — it was DELETED silently, whatever its target was doing, and a clean done home was never evidence the dependency had been met. Only an amended-blocked-by line, which resolves last-wins over the slot and which the move does not touch, rode in alive and produced the unrepairable body. One defect, opposite signs. item close now reads the EFFECTIVE blocker before anything moves: target DONE records blocker-moot, --drop records the wait ABANDONED never answered, anything else on a DONE close REFUSES. ## lc-102
grade: DONE
requirement: the leak scan's fallback scans NO range interior while its own message says it scans everything, and on this machine the fallback is the ordinary path. absence-scan.mjs's EMPTY sentinel walks files with 'ls-tree -r <newRef>' (genuinely the whole tip tree) but walks COMMITS with 'rev-list <newRef> --not --all --not <newRef> --branches --tags --remotes', which resolves to ZERO as soon as any local branch points at the tip - always true on an ordinary push. So a blob added and then REMOVED inside the pushed range is never scanned, the tip tree is clean, and the run prints 'absence-scan: clean'. That is the commonest accidental-leak shape there is: commit a secret, notice, delete it in a later commit of the same push. The degraded line's wording - 'scanning everything at <newRef>' - is an assurance wider than the predicate that backs it, which is what stops anyone looking. SECOND, SAME FILE, SAME CLASS: the USAGE line says '<old> may be EMPTY', which reads as English 'may be blank' and means the literal token - the label that produced lc-89's two defect sites - record: lc-89 lane report 3b, 2026-09-13
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: tools/absence-scan.mjs (LANDS IN claude-code-cache-fix FIRST - this repo's copy is byte-identical and declared as such; the write boundary is cross-repo and is NOT this desk's),test/absence-scan.test.mjs
done-criterion: the fallback either scans the range interior, or says truthfully what it does not scan - the message and the walk agree, whichever way they are made to agree. A verdict line reading clean over an unscanned interior is the defect; a degraded line that names the gap is an acceptable answer and a silent one is not. The USAGE line names the token as a literal. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the tip-tree walk keeps its current reach, and a genuine range with a resolvable base keeps scanning its interior exactly as it does now - this is about the FALLBACK, and widening the fallback must not narrow the normal path
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK against this repo's real history, 2026-09-13, not against a fixture: 'git rev-list HEAD --not --all --not HEAD --branches --tags --remotes' returns 0 commits here while 'git ls-tree -r --name-only HEAD' returns 57 files, and the control 'git rev-list HEAD~3..HEAD' returns 3 - so the walk discriminates and the zero is a real emptiness. Found first by the lc-89 build lane in two purpose-built fixtures, each carrying a blob added-then-removed inside the range: neither spelling of the fallback found it, identically, so lc-89 did not move this and it is not a regression. Every push this desk made today took the fallback path

## lc-91
grade: DONE
requirement: A repo cannot declare its OWN closure vocabulary. lc-19 closed on the core defect - DONE and DROPPED now have classification rules derived from items.GRADES_CLOSED - but the second half of its criterion is unbuilt: GRADES_CLOSED is a fixed tuple in the tool, so a source carrier whose closures read ERLEDIGT, RESOLVED, TRACED or EXECUTED still falls through as unclassified. Those four are not hypothetical: they are the exact words lc-19 own measurement counted in the real dotfiles carriers
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,test/test_migrate.py
done-criterion: a repo can declare a closure-word mapping its migration honours, so a carrier closing entries in its own vocabulary migrates them to the done home rather than unclassified. Red-first on lc-19 own measured words: a fixture whose closures read ERLEDIGT and RESOLVED lands in the done home under a declared mapping and is unclassified without one. MUST-NOT-MOVE, and it is the point lc-19 made explicitly: a word with NO rule is still reported unclassified rather than guessed at - the fix is declared rules, never a looser matcher
evidence: SPLIT OUT of lc-19 at its closure, 2026-09-12 retirement pass, rather than absorbed silently: the closing lane flagged that the criterion said DONE and DROPPED at minimum PLUS a declared way for a repo to map its own closure words, and only the first half shipped in f3f7517. Verified at this desk: CLOSURE_RULES is built from items.GRADES_CLOSED, a fixed two-word tuple. The four foreign closure words are lc-19 own executed counts over the real carriers - root BACKLOG.md ERLEDIGT 1 and RESOLVED 1, claude/BACKLOG.md TRACED 1 and EXECUTED 1
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-13 DECISION MADE AT THIS DESK, not deferred further: a repo declares its closure vocabulary in .claude/lifecycle.json, as a closure-words mapping migrate honours. Derivability, named so the next reader can check the reasoning rather than the verdict: (1) that file is already where every other per-repo constant lives - id-prefix, laws, closure-home, goals, kinds and their homes - so a closure-words list there is the same KIND of fact in the same place, while the other two candidates put it somewhere no other repo-constant lives; (2) a per-migration FLAG would have to be repeated identically at every invocation, which is the mirrored-by-convention shape this family has already paid for once - two places holding one value is drift waiting to happen, and the tool cannot detect the day they diverge; (3) renaming foreign closure words AT THE SOURCE contradicts what the source carrier is for - it is READ and cited by line, and rewriting it moves the very lines the citations pin, which is the defect lc-86 spent today preventing in the other direction. The rejected alternatives are recorded here rather than only in chat because the next reader will otherwise re-derive them. What this does NOT decide, and the build must not assume: the mapping's SHAPE (a flat word-to-grade map, or grade-to-word-list) is the builder's call against items.GRADES_CLOSED's own structure, and the must-not-move stands unchanged - a word with no declared rule is still reported unclassified, never guessed at
amended-blocked-by: 2026-09-13 NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-13 2026-09-13 evidence corrected IN PLACE after measuring, and one argument of my own ruling narrowed. The ruling STANDS — a repo declares its closure vocabulary in .claude/lifecycle.json — on arguments (1) same kind of fact in the same place and (2) a per-migration flag is the mirrored-by-convention drift shape. Argument (3) said renaming foreign closure words AT THE SOURCE 'contradicts what the source carrier is for'. That is too strong and the record refutes it: the operator DID rename all four at the source, by policy. What (3) actually establishes, and it is still sufficient: a migration tool may not REQUIRE a rewrite of a read-only, line-cited carrier as its only path. Recorded here rather than only in chat because the next reader would otherwise re-derive it — and because a decision resting partly on a refuted argument executes stale
amended-evidence: 2026-09-13 SPLIT OUT of lc-19 at its closure, 2026-09-12 retirement pass. Verified at this desk 2026-09-13: CLOSURE_RULES is built from items.GRADES_CLOSED, a fixed two-word tuple ('DONE','DROPPED') at items.py:60, and there are TWO derivation sites in migrate.py, not one — CLOSURE_RULES at :156-163 and the _CLOSURE_WORD regex at :249-251, whose own comment says 'DERIVED from items.GRADES_CLOSED, like CLOSURE_RULES — one vocabulary, one home'. A change reaching only the first leaves the second blind. THE FOUR FOREIGN WORDS ARE HISTORY, NOT PRESENT STATE, and the earlier evidence line read as present: dotfiles cbe33d5 remapped all four at the source on 2026-08-27 under the everything-English operator decision — ERLEDIGT/RESOLVED/EXECUTED under Done became DONE, the open TRACED record became PARKED — so at the blobs the dotfiles deletion record pins, TRACED appears 0 times in either carrier and EXECUTED 0 times in claude/BACKLOG.md. Instrument limit stated so the next reader does not over-read it: a whole-file grep -cw counts LINES CONTAINING a word, not grade words in GRADE POSITION, so these counts are not lc-19's instrument and do not refute its measurement — they establish only the one-directional fact that a word absent from a file cannot be a grade in it. THE DEFECT IS UNTOUCHED: lifecycle is a PLUGIN, and a repo closing entries in its own vocabulary still migrates them unclassified. The motivation is other repos, not this one
closed-reason: 2026-09-13 A repo declares closure-words in .claude/lifecycle.json — flat word->grade, OPTIONAL — and migrate honours it by TRANSLATING AT INTAKE, so GRADES_CLOSED stays the two-word tuple and items.py is untouched. Both derivation sites reached: the bullet-start lookup and closure_word_in_title's scan, so a declared closure late in an ungraded title is AMBIGUOUS exactly as a native one is rather than silently becoming open NEW work. CASE-SENSITIVITY, the one decision left open, was settled by the lane from _GRADE_WORD's own ^([A-Z][A-Z0-9-]*[A-Z0-9])(?![a-z]) — matching is case-sensitive, and a lower- or mixed-case declared word is REFUSED AT DECLARATION TIME rather than sitting in the file reading live and firing never: an unfalsifiable check caught before it was written. MUST-NOT-MOVE held: an undeclared word is still UNCLASSIFIED, in the same run whose declared closure moved; a repo declaring nothing behaves exactly as before; the key is not in REQUIRED_KEYS so no schema bump. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK INDEPENDENTLY, all five suites on the LIVE working copy: unittest 395 OK (379+16, 0 skipped), --test 78/78 CLEAN exit 0, audit exit 3, node 62/61/1 (documented foreign-tree bite), prove-rows exit 3 with 63 PROVEN and zero FAIL. Both anchored rows survive the change — migration_ambiguous_closure PROVEN and migration_unclassified PROVEN — which was the stated condition, since the fallback design fires precisely when that row's mutation disables the native scan. My live prover run also closes the lane's own not-verified item: it could only run against a snapshot, because a DEAD co-lane's stale writer-reservation still named this copy as held. One brief defect found by the lane and recorded against me: my consumer table was built by grepping a token, so it could not surface a third prove-rows anchor that does not contain that token; the sweep keyed on the invariant finds five, not one.

## lc-103
grade: DONE
requirement: this repo ships git hooks and has no guard that they stay LAUNCHABLE. A hook committed without its executable bit is a gate that fails OPEN - git cannot run it, the push proceeds, and every content check reports clean because the bytes are right. It happened here: 0cbd1ad committed tools/git-hooks/pre-push at mode 100644 with the SAME blob sha as the working file, so this repo's leak scan was a dead gate until d8c3934 repaired the mode. The cause generalizes past the incident: a Python file write drops the source's mode, and 'git status --porcelain' prints one ' M' for a mode change on an already-modified file, so the mode change hides inside the content change and the commit summary line is the first instrument that names it - after the commit. dotfiles guards this class twice over (pre-commit nonexec_hook_commands, doctor check_plugin_hook_files); this repo, which declares git-hooks in its own plugin.json, guards it not at all - record: lc-89 lane report 3a, 2026-09-13
goal: every-refusal-red-first
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,test/
done-criterion: a hook this repo ships cannot be committed or pass its own checks without the executable bit: the mode of every declared git-hook path is asserted against git's own record (git ls-tree's mode, not the filesystem alone, since a deployed symlink's target is what git stores), and the refusal is a registered roster row with plant AND control. Red-first is already in hand and must be reproduced: 0cbd1ad is a real commit in this history carrying mode 100644 on a hook, so the check goes red against that ref and green against d8c3934. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a NON-hook file's mode is not policed - this is about artifacts something launches, and a guard that fires on ordinary files trains the override reflex that kills it
evidence: MEASURED AT THIS DESK, 2026-09-13, independently of the lane's report: 'git ls-tree 0cbd1ad -- tools/git-hooks/pre-push' returns mode 100644 and 'git ls-tree HEAD' returns 100755, with the SAME blob sha 887ecff8 in both - content identical, mode alone differing, which is exactly why a sha-only restore check passed it. The dead-gate consequence was measured by the lane at the artifact git actually runs: the .git/hooks/pre-push symlink answered 'permission denied'. The dotfiles-side guards are named from that repo's CLAUDE.md, RELAYED and not re-read here
blocked-by: NONE
closed-reason: 2026-09-13 kind check now asserts the COMMITTED mode of every git hook this repo ships, so a hook that cannot be launched is a finding rather than a gate that fails open. The population is DERIVED every run as the ruling's union — plugin.json's git-hooks scripts resolved against the plugin tree and kept only where they lie inside the repo under test, UNION every file directly under tools/git-hooks/ — never the declaration alone, which was the spec gap this desk caught at brief time: plugin.json declares only pre-commit, so a guard keyed on it would have been GREEN over this repo's own motivating incident. Mode from git ls-tree, never stat, because a deployed hook is reached through a symlink and the COMMITTED mode is what a fresh clone gets. THE PROOF IS THE REPO'S OWN HISTORY AND IT IS NOW A TEST, not a measurement taken once: at 0cbd1ad the guard FINDS (exit 2, exactly one finding asserted, so nothing but the mode could move it) and at d8c3934 it is CLEAN (exit 0) — SAME blob 887ecff8, mode the only difference — with two arrangement guards riding along so the proof cannot decay silently: the refs still resolve, and they still carry one blob at two modes. Verified independently at this desk before the lane's report: unittest 405 OK, --test 80/80 CLEAN exit 0, audit exit 3, node 62/61/1 (documented bite), prove-rows exit 3 with 64 PROVEN and zero FAIL after I landed the mutation entry, all 13 declaration.py anchors intact at count 1. The lane and I chose the same mutation anchor and replacement independently. THREE THINGS IT SURFACED THAT OUTLIVE IT: lc-107 (audit and retire DISCARD every declaration finding on the readable path — found while ruling out a collision I had asked about, and worse than the collision), lc-108 (the CLEAN line states no count, so an empty guarded set reads as checked-and-clean — the lane HALTED at the write boundary rather than designing around it), and the devbook lesson that one git update-index --refresh is necessary but not sufficient before reading git status, which produced a false alarm at this desk on the same afternoon.
closed-ref: 2b9fe40, 2915be1, 6729b58

## lc-10
grade: DONE
requirement: §3.11's intake cost test has three conjuncts and the third is unimplemented: cost_test() never receives the typed blocker — record: wave2 L1 booking run, 2026-08-26
goal: every-refusal-red-first
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: an item with a typed decision blocker and a one-file write-set is graded NEW without the do-it-now ask, red-first against the current implementation
evidence: verbs.py:268 signature is cost_test(write_set, hunks, source) — no blocker parameter, and its docstring cites §3.2 not §3.11; observed live when a booking carrying a typed decision blocker was held for a hunk count
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-12 2026-09-12 Second live firing, from another desk and another repo, plus one consequence the entry did not carry. Also a correction to the diagnosis I arrived with: I came to book 'the cost test is blind to cross-repo write boundaries' as a new item. That framing is WRONG and this entry already has it right — the declared rule (declaration.py, intake-cost-test) reads 'write-set <= 1 file AND session live AND NO TYPED BLOCKER -> ask do-it-now; any other shape -> NEW', so a typed blocker is ALREADY the exemption the spec grants, and a cross-repo write boundary is simply one thing a typed blocker expresses. Inventing a second mechanism beside a correctly-diagnosed one would have split the fix across two entries and left this one looking narrower than it is.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-12 an item with a typed decision blocker and a one-file write-set is graded NEW without the do-it-now ask, red-first against the current implementation — AND the add->parked path exists end to end: a cross-boundary item whose realizing write is another desk's can be created in one verb, parked, with its typed blocker intact. Red-first arrangement: run the NEW expectations against the OLD implementation, with the unmutated baseline run first and its result stated, since a mutate-and-revert proof over an already-red baseline proves nothing. Both firings above are the cases-in-hand the criterion is dry-run against: the 2026-08-26 wave2 booking and the 2026-09-12 dotfiles cross-repo booking.
amended-evidence: 2026-09-12 PREMISE RE-READ AND STILL HOLDING at 2026-09-12: the signature is cost_test(write_set, hunks, source) with no blocker parameter, now at plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py:347 (the entry cites :268 — the code moved, the defect did not; citation refreshed here so it resolves). Its sole call site is verbs.py:806, inside the add verb, where slots['blocked-by'] is in scope and simply is not passed. SECOND LIVE OCCURRENCE, 2026-09-12, dotfiles drainage desk: an  in dotfiles carrying --blocked-by 'decision when the judgment desk lands its bundled dispatch-guards corpus queue' — a correctly typed decision blocker — was vetoed with cost_test_veto, TWICE, on a one-file one-hunk write-set naming another repo's file. Under the declared rule that add should have graded NEW without the ask. First occurrence (wave2 L1, 2026-08-26) was the same shape, so n=2 across two desks and two repos. CONSEQUENCE THE ENTRY DID NOT CARRY, and it is what makes the defect a dead end rather than an annoyance: when the veto fires on a legitimately blocked item there is NO CORRECT VERB LEFT.  takes an existing ident and re-grades it, so it cannot create the parked item;  refuses it; and the refusal's own two offered exits are both wrong here — inflating --hunks is the override reflex the corpus forbids, and --source operator writes FALSE PROVENANCE whenever the asker was a peer desk rather than the operator, since a delegation does not convert a desk's ask into the operator's decision. So the author's only compliant move is to not book the item at all, which is the silent loss the two-exits rule exists to prevent. That third exit is what the fix must restore, not merely the missing parameter.
amend-reason: 2026-09-12 2026-09-12 REPAIR of the amendment immediately above, which landed corrupted by my own shell error, not by any judgment change. I wrapped verb names in backticks inside a zsh argument; zsh ran them as command substitution (three 'command not found: item' lines in the output), so each backticked phrase was replaced by the EMPTY STRING before the text ever reached the carrier. The previous amended-evidence therefore reads 'an  in dotfiles carrying', '  takes an existing ident' and ' refuses it' — three sentences with their subjects deleted. The content below is that same evidence with the verb names written as plain text. Recorded rather than silently re-amended: the corruption is a live instance of the class this carrier keeps, and an amendment whose damage is invisible in the rendered line is exactly the paraphrase-drift shape.
amended-evidence: 2026-09-12 PREMISE RE-READ AND STILL HOLDING at 2026-09-12: the signature is cost_test(write_set, hunks, source) with no blocker parameter, now at plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py:347 — the entry originally cited :268, so the code moved and the defect did not; citation refreshed here so it resolves. Its sole call site is verbs.py:806, inside the add verb, where slots['blocked-by'] is in scope and simply is not passed. SECOND LIVE OCCURRENCE, 2026-09-12, dotfiles drainage desk: an ITEM ADD in dotfiles carrying --blocked-by 'decision when the judgment desk lands its bundled dispatch-guards corpus queue' — a correctly typed decision blocker — was vetoed with cost_test_veto, TWICE, on a one-file one-hunk write-set naming another repo's file. Under the declared rule (declaration.py, intake-cost-test: write-set <= 1 file AND session live AND no typed blocker -> ask; any other shape -> NEW) that add should have graded NEW without the ask. The first occurrence, wave2 L1 on 2026-08-26, was the same shape: n=2 across two desks and two repos. CONSEQUENCE THE ENTRY DID NOT CARRY, and it is what turns this from an annoyance into a dead end: when the veto fires on a legitimately blocked item there is NO CORRECT VERB LEFT. ITEM PARK takes an existing ident and re-grades it, so it cannot create the parked item; ITEM ADD refuses it; and the refusal's own two offered exits are both wrong here — inflating --hunks is the override reflex the corpus forbids, and --source operator writes FALSE PROVENANCE whenever the asker was a peer desk rather than the operator, since a delegation does not convert a desk's ask into the operator's decision. The author's only compliant move is then to not book the item at all, which is precisely the silent loss the two-exits rule exists to prevent. Restoring that third exit is part of the fix, not merely passing the missing parameter.
amend-reason: 2026-09-13 2026-09-13 citation corrected in the evidence slot (the wrong pointer sat in a prior amend-reason, which is not an amendable slot) and the desk's own premise re-read folded in. Found by the build lane's grounding round
amended-evidence: 2026-09-13 PREMISES RE-READ AT THIS DESK 2026-09-13 at c8f3e84, both holding: cost_test(write_set, hunks, source) at plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py:347 still takes no blocker, and its SOLE call site verbs.py:806 is 'cost_test(slots["write-set"], args.hunks, source)' with slots plainly in scope. Two live firings stand as recorded: wave2 L1 2026-08-26, and the dotfiles cross-repo booking 2026-09-12 vetoed twice on a one-file one-hunk write-set carrying a correctly typed decision blocker — n=2 across two desks and two repos. THE CONSEQUENCE is what makes it a dead end: when the veto fires on a legitimately blocked item there is no correct verb left — amend takes an existing ident so it cannot create the parked item, add refuses, and the refusal's two offered exits are both wrong (inflating --hunks is the override reflex; --source operator writes FALSE PROVENANCE when the asker was a peer desk, since a delegation does not convert a desk's ask into the operator's decision). CITATION CORRECTED, found by the lc-10 build lane and verified here: the declared rule is NOT in declaration.py, which has zero hits for 'intake-cost-test'. It is judgment.py:89 — Rule(ident='intake-cost-test', statement='write-set <= 1 file AND session live AND no typed blocker -> the tool asks "do it now?"; any other shape -> NEW. Never a silent decision either way.', site='verbs.cost_test', row='cost_test_veto'). The quoted text was verbatim right, so the diagnosis was never in doubt; only the pointer was wrong, and it propagated into this desk's brief because the entry's citation was carried forward without being opened — proving a reference resolves is not opening its content. The rule's own notes carry the half with a mechanism: an add naming one file and stating no hunk count is COULD NOT VERIFY, never a pass, because a cost test that silently cleared what it could not evaluate would clear exactly the adds worth vetoing
closed-reason: 2026-09-13 The intake cost test's third conjunct is implemented: cost_test now receives the typed blocker the declared rule already names, so a correctly-blocked one-file item is graded NEW without the do-it-now ask. The fix is ADDITIVE around two prove-rows anchors that sit INSIDE cost_test itself (cost_test_unverified :362, cost_test_veto :375) — neither restructured, the new branch placed AHEAD of the hunk question deliberately, because with a typed blocker the conjunction is already false and demanding a hunk count that cannot change the verdict is what closed the last exit. The blocker is classified BY THE CALLER, reusing the classify_blocker call add already makes, and the parameter is required rather than defaulted since a default reinstates the defect. WHAT THIS RESTORES IS THE THIRD EXIT: a cross-boundary item whose realizing write is another desk's can now be created in ONE verb, parked, blocker intact — where before there was no correct verb at all, and the refusal's two offered exits were both wrong (inflating --hunks is the override reflex; --source operator writes false provenance when a peer desk asked). VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY AT THIS DESK on the live copy: unittest 411 OK (405+6, 0 skipped), --test 80/80 CLEAN exit 0, audit exit 3, node 62/61/1 documented bite, prove-rows exit 3 with 64 PROVEN and zero FAIL — and all three at-risk rows still PROVEN (cost_test_veto, cost_test_unverified, new_without_typed_blocker). Anchor sweep 21/21 exactly once before and after, by the lane and again here. Red-first was failures=3 errors=0 at the CLI altitude, kept there deliberately because a unit-level arm would red as TypeError and score identically against a build that takes the argument and ignores it. THREE THINGS IT SURFACED: lc-109 (the override write is decided by a substring match over a rendered message, so wording can book a false override into the register that prices retirement), lc-110 (the register now has a third outcome nobody counts, so fire rate is priced on a denominator omitting the new commonest path — ruled COUNT IT), and a devbook lesson that a co-writer WRITING inside a bite window invalidates the bite silently, whose incident was this desk running the prover on the live tree while the lane worked. Citation corrected in the evidence slot: the declared rule is judgment.py:89, not declaration.py, which has zero hits.

MOVERS, n=3, all below the edit: df-3 cites :64, df-19 cites :553, df-31 cites :976 — under the LIVE file each lands mid-body (wrong); under the pin each lands exactly on its entry's opening bullet (right). MUST-NOT-MOVE control: line 10, above the edit site, is byte-identical live and pinned — so the pin is not merely shifting every line. ## lc-115
grade: DONE
requirement: test/test_hook_modes.py has no git-presence guard, so in a git archive snapshot — which the devbook now MANDATES for any mutate-and-restore work — it produces reds and errors that belong to the ARRANGEMENT, not the code. Two lanes have now spent attention diagnosing them, and the live risk is a third lane 'repairing' them and silencing a correct instrument — record: lc-12 and lc-38 lanes, 2026-09-13
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: test/test_hook_modes.py
done-criterion: the class detects an absent repository and SKIPS with an explicit reason naming the arrangement, rather than failing or erroring — the THIRD answer, which the file's two-answer version already reaches for. Red-first: run the class inside a git archive HEAD snapshot and show today's failures=2 errors=3; green-after: the same run reports skips with reasons and zero failures and zero errors. MUST-NOT-MOVE, and it is the whole risk of this repair: inside a REAL repository every case must still RUN and still be able to go red — a guard that skips too eagerly converts the repo's own recorded instance of the defect into a permanent silent pass, which is strictly worse than the noise it removes. Assert the not-skipped path explicitly in-repo.
evidence: MEASURED BY THE lc-38 LANE and reported to this desk, graded as its measurement: a git archive HEAD snapshot of unmodified HEAD runs 'Ran 426 tests' -> 'FAILED (failures=2, errors=3, skipped=1)', every red and the single skip inside test_hook_modes, because TheRepoSOwnRecordedInstance pins real refs (0cbd1ad, d8c3934) and reads git mode bits while 'test -d .git' is absent. SECOND LANE, same day: lc-12 reported 'The snapshot's test_hook_modes reds I attributed to the missing .git, no further' — so n=2 lanes, independently, each correctly diagnosing and each paying for it. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK by reading the file: the class clones from REPO_ROOT with subprocess check=True (test_hook_modes.py:272-277), so an absent repo raises before any assertion, and a grep for skipTest/skipUnless/exists across the file returns NOTHING — there is no git-presence guard at any altitude. SHARPEST GROUNDING, from the file's own words: test_the_refs_this_proof_is_pinned_to_still_resolve exists precisely because 'a ref that no longer resolves turns every assertion below into a setup error wearing a finding's clothes' — the right instinct, guarding the ref case and leaving the no-repo case open. PROVENANCE NOTE: this desk commissioned that test (lc-103) and specified those pinned refs, so the gap is the dispatcher's, not the lane's.

Design was the lane's: a _NeedsThisRepo mixin whose setUpClass skips, one body mixed in rather than a precondition restated per class — a restatement drifts and the class keeping the old spelling silently returns to erroring. The predicate asks git for --show-toplevel and COMPARES it to REPO_ROOT rather than testing for .git, because rev-parse walks UPWARDS: a snapshot extracted inside another repository would otherwise answer about that one. Bite C proved that branch load-bearing by injecting the comparison out and reproducing the original defect signature exactly. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK, not from the report: live 429 OK exit 0; snapshot of ed0066d 424 OK skipped=4, zero failures zero errors. I reconciled the 429-vs-424 gap myself because four skips do not explain five missing tests — diffed the collected sets and the five absent are EXACTLY the five guarded cases (Population x2, TheRepoSOwnRecordedInstance x3), nothing silently uncollected; a setUpClass skip does not increment the per-test total, which is why five tests hide behind four skip entries. Skip reasons print and name the arrangement. The must-not-move arm deliberately does NOT carry the guard it grades — it asks the filesystem for .git, a second instrument an over-firing predicate cannot silence — and the lane MEASURED why that matters: same injection, mixin added back, gives OK skipped=3 exit 0, a silent green over a swallowed proof. RESIDUE RECORDED, honest: the arm catches OVER-firing, not under-firing; a corrupt repo answering .git-present-but-git-declines would red rather than skip, reasoned not exercised; and the lane did NOT run the snapshot copy's own --test self-check (devbook step 2's letter), substituting 423 green tests around the five reds — substance met, letter partially. One incident, self-caught: a whole-file write dropped the _Fixture class, found by reading the DELETION side of the diff against HEAD before running anything, restored byte-identically within about one tool round; a co-writer running that file inside the window would have seen NameError reds, and nobody can prove none did.

## lc-77
grade: DONE
requirement: No verb reports an item's EFFECTIVE slot values. This carrier's edit path is append-and-supersede by design: item amend writes a dated amended-<slot> line and retains the earlier one, item promote writes promoted-by and promote-reason, so a slot's effective value is the LAST amended-<slot> line where one exists and the base slot otherwise. Every reader outside the tool re-derives that rule by hand, and a hand-derived rule is what drifts. record: retirement pass R1 dispatch, 2026-09-12
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: a READ-ONLY verb prints each item's effective slot values with the supersession rule applied (last amended-<slot> wins, base slot otherwise), in a form another program can consume. Red-first on an item carrying TWO amendments to ONE slot, asserting the LAST is reported and the first is not: lc-7 in this carrier is that case today, and lc-75 carried amended-done-criterion before this pass, so the red is drawn from the real carrier rather than a fixture. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the verb writes nothing at all (no carrier write, no commit, no lock), and an item with no amendments reports its base slots byte-unchanged. DESIGN CONSTRAINT (operator, first-hand, binding on this entry): this verb is what makes POSITIONAL slot anchoring possible for a future kind's checker, so it reads slots by position in the block and never by matching a word anywhere in free prose, which is the failure the backlog doctrine names.
evidence: Twice-written, which is this repo's own graduation threshold for a probe (the repo-tools kind: a probe used twice graduates or dies). The dispatching desk reports writing this extractor twice as throwaway across three repos in this wave. The rule it must encode is live in the real carrier rather than hypothetical: lc-7 carries TWO amended-evidence lines written minutes apart in this pass, the second correcting the first's basis, and lc-75 already carried amended-done-criterion at f09e32d. The rule also already exists inside the tool, in items.py _resolve_amendments, so the verb EXPOSES an existing rule rather than minting a second body for it, which is what would otherwise disagree first.
blocked-by: NONE
blocker-moot: the verb's spelling and output form: a new item slots verb versus a flag on item ready, plain lines versus a machine-readable mode, and whether it reports one item or the whole carrier. This repo has been bitten by an unspelled verb before (the dropped lc-2), so the spelling is the desk's call
closed-reason: 2026-09-13 Scheduled early on the judgment desk's pointer because the trap it retires had just bitten this desk: the wave-1 write-set join read base slots and mis-laned lc-60, whose amendment moves it into the core component. Verified on exactly that case — 'item slots lc-60' returns the amended five-file set, not the three-file base. The verb exposes _resolve_amendments rather than reimplementing it, and reads slots by POSITION per the operator's binding constraint. Red-first on the real carrier (lc-7 two same-slot amendments, lc-22 none), failures=1 errors=0 on both, and the lc-7 discriminator was itself checked for discriminating power: its two amended-evidence lines differ (832 vs 1106 bytes, diverging at char 554), so last-wins is a real assertion and not a vacuous one. Verified outside the codex sandbox: 480 OK, --test 82/82 CLEAN, git status byte-identical across plain and --json runs, no lock created. ACCEPTED DEVIATION: the brief's 'git status must be empty' must-not-move was unsatisfiable inside the lane's own write set; the lane proved the meaningful invariant (status unchanged BY the verb) and reported the defect rather than bending to it — the brief was wrong, not the lane. RESIDUE the lane surfaced and this desk has NOT acted on: same-date amendments must be resolved by FILE ORDER, not by date sorting, since lc-7's two amendments share a date — true of the current resolver, untested against a future one.

## lc-22
grade: DONE
requirement: The closure MOVE has no guard against carrying a LIVE obligation into an archive. An entry can name itself the carrier for a pointer another desk still owes — 'this entry is the carrier that moves with it', its own words — and when its own work closes, the move takes that clause into the done home, which is headed for pruning at reviews. The obligation then reads as closed because its carrier is filed as closed. `item close` should grep the entry body for a forward-carrier clause and REFUSE the move with the clause quoted, so a human splits the residue out first.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: red-first against the L10 entry's body at dotfiles bb8edd4: `item close` refuses and quotes the carrier clause. Green once the clause is gone or the residue has its own entry. AND the over-fire arm, which decides whether this is shippable at all: an ordinary entry that merely DISCUSSES carriers or pointers in its prose must NOT be refused — a phrase-matching predicate over free prose is exactly the shape that fires on legitimate work, so the match anchors on a declared clause form rather than on words occurring anywhere in the body.
evidence: the L10 case at the dotfiles desk, 2026-08-26: the corpus-consolidation entry named itself the carrier for this desk's accretion-module residue and then closed. The peer halted the move by hand, took it to the judgment desk, and the residue was split into its own entry (dotfiles e3b3ebf) — a hand catch where a mechanism should have refused. Clause text is the peer's quotation, not read here; the entry body at dotfiles bb8edd4 is the red-first input. Booked on the judgment desk's instruction.

closed-reason: 2026-09-15 item close now refuses a move that would file a DECLARED forward-carrier clause, anchored on the uppercase CARRIED POINTER marker (one spelling, no synonyms, no bypass flag), registered as roster row close_carries_pointer with an executable plant and control. HELD after the build lane's own post-report finding (law 21) and released only after the repair landed. THE HOLD'S ORIGINAL BASIS WAS WRONG and is recorded as such: the claim that no committed fixture placed the marker mid-line, so a line-anchored predicate would pass the whole battery, was REFUTED by the repair lane before its first build call — the predicate reads the LINE, _clause_block renders 'requirement: {value}', so every fixture line already carried the property. Measured: the line-anchored twin reds the unrepaired battery at failures=4, errors=0, and change 1 as originally specced left that outcome bit-identical. The desk's own confirmation of the false claim had reproduced the originating lane's framing (value-start, not line-start) rather than testing the predicate's actual input. THE SURVIVING DEFECT WAS NARROWER: the corpus over-fire arm's POSITIVE CONTROL was handed the bare constant, marker at column 0 — the only fixture blind to a line-anchored predicate, i.e. a control that could not see the property it certified. Repaired at the call site (constant untouched) plus a named mid-line arm carrying its own pair. Verified at this desk independently, not booked on report: red-first pair in an isolated clone integrity-checked against committed blobs — baseline 60 OK; line-anchored + unrepaired FAILED failures=4 with the corpus arm PASSING (the defect); line-anchored + repaired FAILED failures=5 with the corpus arm failing on its own 'positive control silent — the instrument is dead' message; unmutated + repaired 60 OK. prove-rows re-run by the desk at HEAD: exit 0, [close_carries_pointer] PROVEN, rows changed: close_carries_pointer, 69 of 84, every recorded arrangement held; the recorded anchor 'if clauses:' still unique in verbs.py after lc-55's commits into the same function. Over-fire at the desk: 74 live bodies scanned, 0 would refuse, positive control firing in the same run. RESIDUAL, named not closed: the clearance route the refusal names is executable by no verb (booked lc-131); the marker is matched per LINE, so a clause wrapped across lines is not matched — harmless only because the carrier's own shape rule refuses wrapped slot values, read but not separately exercised; the predicate establishes the clause's PRESENCE and never that the obligation is still live, which the row text says in its own words.

grade: DONE
requirement: `item repair --shape` reports its judgment classes in a SECOND VOCABULARY over findings the registry already carries, and nothing checks that the two agree. So these are not unregistered findings, which is how the lc-130 lane framed it in its gap 2; they are one fact under two names. The two axes are defensible on their own — a row says WHAT IS WRONG, a JUDGMENT class says WHAT THE VERB DECLINED TO REPAIR AND WHY — but nothing holds them in correspondence, so a row added to the checker with no judgment class, or a class added with no row, drifts silently and the verb's list stops reconciling with the checker's residue. grade: DONE
requirement: tools/prove-rows.py RESTORE IS NOT CRASH-SAFE: it mutates a core file, runs the roster, and restores by file copy at the END of each arm — so a run interrupted mid-arm (SIGKILL, a harness timeout, an operator Ctrl-C) leaves the injected mutation LIVE in the tree with no marker that it did. SHARPER than the known run-it-in-a-private-copy hazard: a lane that obeys the venue rule and is then interrupted still leaves a mutated tree, so the venue rule is load-bearing rather than merely tidy — record: lc-128 lane closing analysis plus desk incident, 2026-09-15

Desk measurement: the killed run's residue was a single line in migrate.py, tree otherwise clean; restored from the committed blob (never git checkout --), sha256-verified equal at 0c9f1ef5a92f8b1c. A parallel lane independently confirmed the battery DISCRIMINATES that exact mutation (failures=6, errors=0), so the residue was detectable — just not by anything that was looking

The dispatch discipline names exactly this — the co-writer these rules under-bind is the DISPATCHER, who reads itself as the boundary's author rather than a party inside it. ## lc-141
grade: DONE
requirement: This repo already HAS the correct instrument for reading an item and nothing points a brief-writer at it, so consumers hand-roll a block slicer instead and the slicer truncates. item slots <id> emits the slots with amendments already RESOLVED — the current truth, no amendment lines to miss. A hand-rolled read of ITEMS.md gets the base slots and drops whatever sits below its window, and amendment slots sit at the END of a block by construction, so a truncating read drops exactly the lines that supersede. Three stale Background passages shipped in one dispatch brief this way. Record: drain wave 1, lanes A and B critique passes, 2026-09-15.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: CLAUDE.md,JOURNAL.md
done-criterion: The repo laws carry the two-instrument rule with its journal pointer, per this repo's own standard that a law without a journal pointer has no basis. RULE TEXT, designed here so it is not re-derived: an item is read for its CURRENT truth by item slots <id>, which resolves amendments; the RAW block is read when the amendment HISTORY is what is wanted, because item slots drops amend-reason lines and those carry the why — measured on lc-47, whose deadlock-dissolution reasoning lives only in an amend-reason. A hand-rolled slice of ITEMS.md is neither and is refused as a reading method. Done when the law is in CLAUDE.md with its JOURNAL entry, and the JOURNAL entry carries the incident below. NOT a checker: no computable predicate distinguishes a hand-rolled read from any other file read, so this is prose-rest and says so — mechanizing it would need a guard over how a session reads a file, which does not exist.
evidence: Measured in operation 2026-09-15, drain wave 1, desk dotfiles-f1. The desk composed lane briefs from a hand-rolled block extractor truncating at ~1600 chars; the four item bodies involved run to 2253 (lc-51), 2785 (lc-132), 3242 (lc-133) and 3423 (lc-30) chars. Three Background passages shipped stale: lc-132 cited two stderr sites both false against an amendment naming the real single site, lc-133 commissioned a red for a try/finally half already in live code, lc-30 commissioned a red on a live instance already repaired. Lane A and lane B each returned one in their critique pass and built against the item bodies correctly; the desk then swept all eleven wave and held items and found no fourth live instance. VERIFIED AT THE ARTIFACT, and it refuted the desk's first framing: the desk was about to book this as a MISSING verb, ran item slots lc-132 before claiming the absence, and found the verb exists AND had already resolved the amendment — its evidence line printed the corrected text, not the original. So the gap is discoverability and discipline, not capability, and the booking changed shape before it was written.

closed-reason: 2026-09-15 2026-09-15 Law 8 widened to name the tool as its carriers' authoritative READER, not only their only writer: an item's CURRENT truth comes from `item slots <id>`, which resolves amendments, and its amendment HISTORY from the raw block, which item slots drops — both reads named because neither is sufficient. Journal basis J23. BUILT AT THE DESK, NOT DISPATCHED, and the reason is the devbook's own box: no executor-designed rule text. Handing a lane a law to compose breaks that bar; handing it byte-exact text the desk wrote makes the lane a typist. Same call as law 24. WIDENED RATHER THAN ADDED: law 8 already owned the tool's relationship to its carriers on the writing side, so this is its reading mirror and the file gains no 26th law. THE CLAIM IN THE LAW WAS VERIFIED WITH A CONTROL RATHER THAN ASSERTED: `item slots lc-34` yields 0 amend-reason lines while ITEMS.md carries 55, so the pattern can match and the zero is a real absence rather than a dead search. WHAT THE ENTRY GOT RIGHT AND THE DESK ALMOST DID NOT: this was nearly booked as a MISSING VERB. Running `item slots lc-132` before claiming the absence found the verb already exists AND had already resolved the amendment, so the gap was discoverability rather than capability and the booking changed shape before it was written — the instrument rule paying for itself at the booking moment. LABELLED PROSE-REST IN THE LAW TEXT ITSELF: no computable predicate distinguishes a hand-rolled carrier read from any other file read, so there is nothing to hook. The detector that actually fired in wave 1 was an executing lane opening the body its brief had summarised, which argues for keeping the critique pass commissioned rather than for believing a lint could replace it. NAMED RESIDUE: the half of J23 that cost most — a precedent living only in an amend-reason (a previous desk's refusals.py cohort check, which would have prevented three lanes of wave-1 rework) — is addressed by the law telling readers to read both, and by nothing mechanical.

GROUNDED AT THE ARTIFACT 2026-09-13: lc-7 carries exactly two amended-evidence lines, 832 and 1106 bytes, diverging at char 554, and BOTH are dated 2026-09-12 — verified by parsing ITEMS.md and hashing the two lines at the drain desk. That is what makes lc-7 a genuine discriminator for last-wins and a NON-discriminator for file-order-versus-date-order. The property was already true (_resolve_amendments iterates item.amendments in file-append order and its own docstring says a sort by date would put them in an order nobody wrote), so this is the devbook's already-true-property branch and no production code was touched. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK BY MY OWN MUTATION, not on the lane's report: in a clone, the resolver was mutated to apply amendments in DATE order (sorted by the date embedded in each raw line), the mutation confirmed on disk first (sha 0f335a95 -> a5984ef4, the sorted() line read back present), and the Amendments class went FAILED failures=1 errors=0 — an assertion failure at the defect. ## lc-144
grade: DONE
requirement: tools/prove-rows.py has NO automated test coverage at all — grepped across test/ and plugin/ by the lc-133 lane and confirmed — so lc-133's startup refusal is proven only by an executed CLI pair that does not re-run. A check with no automated home decays silently, and this one guards the tool that REWRITES CORE FILES and restores them: if the refusal regresses, prove-rows resumes capturing a dirty working state as the 'original' and writing it back as such, which is the incident lc-133 was booked from. Record: opus-lc133-51-30 closing report slot (g), 2026-09-15.
goal: every-refusal-red-first
write-set: test/test_prove_rows.py
done-criterion: An automated arm covers prove-rows' startup refusal as a DISCRIMINATING PAIR, not a single assertion: a fixture whose mutation target is dirty gets exit FINDING with the file named, and the SAME fixture clean proceeds — the arms differing in the dirtiness alone, so neither the tool nor the fixture is what separates them. The dirty arm asserts on the refusal's MESSAGE TEXT (the named path and the sha mismatch), never on the exit code alone, because prove-rows exits non-zero for several reasons and a code-only assertion cannot say which fired. RED-FIRST against the pre-lc-133 build at b387ef0, where no refusal exists: the dirty arm must FAIL there as an assertion failure, not error — a fixture reaching through a name the old side lacks proves only that the code is new. ALSO COVER the spec gap lc-133's lane decided rather than inherited: a file ABSENT from HEAD counts as differing and refuses. ## lc-47
grade: DONE
requirement: the lc-44 ruling keeps a DROP reason in the LEDGER while a DONE reason goes in the MOVED BODY, and its stated ground is that a dropped body MAY BE PRUNED so its record cannot live only there. The retire lane design of record (cache-fix carrier-rework-design 3.1) specifies a COMPACTION step collapsing done bodies older than N days to one ledger line each, git keeping the body. Once that exists the pruning argument reaches DONE bodies identically: a compacted body takes its closed-reason and closed-ref with it, and the closure record the doctrine calls load-bearing is gone from every carrier a reader loads. Found by the lc-44 lane while building to the ruling; the ruling is correct TODAY because retire.py has no verb for compaction yet.
goal: one-home-per-kind
write-set: whichever change introduces the compaction verb in retire.py, plus the lc-44 slots in items.py if the answer moves them
done-criterion: the compaction verb ships only WITH an answer to where a compacted item closure record lives. THE QUESTION, so it is not re-derived: compaction turns the moved body into a ledger line, so either the closure lines are LIFTED into that ledger line (one fact still one home, the home changing at compaction time) or DONE bodies carrying closure lines are EXEMPT from compaction (the record outlives the body, at the cost of the carrier not shrinking where it most would). Whichever is chosen, the verb REFUSES to compact a body it would silently strip: red-first is compacting a body carrying closed-reason and closed-ref and showing the record survives in whatever home the answer names. Must-not-move: a DROP still keeps exactly one ledger line and no second copy.
evidence: lc-44 lane interim 3, 2026-08-27, citing cache-fix carrier-rework-design 3.1 and retire.py stating that never, compact and delete have no verb yet; the lc-44 ruling itself (judgment desk, 2026-08-27) for the pruning ground it rests on.

NEITHER of the two branches this entry named: compaction collapses a done body to a ledger line carrying the item id, the closed-ref, AND THE BLOB SHA OF THE DONE CARRIER AT CLOSURE TIME, so the full closed-reason stays recoverable by `git cat-file -p <blob>` while the carrier actually shrinks. Invariant 3 holds — one home, moving from the carrier to git-at-a-pin in one step, never two live copies. Invariant 5 holds — the exit names its commit and its reason is resolvable. THE PIN IS THE VINTAGE BLOB, NEVER HEAD: the sibling repo measured the alternative and recorded it (dotfiles CLAUDE.md:598, the claude-code-cache-fix case where 313 of 318 pointers landed on the wrong entry and NOTHING FAILED, because a line number always resolves). A compaction that pins HEAD is therefore worse than one that pins nothing: it manufactures confident wrong pointers. RED-FIRST: compact a body carrying closed-reason and closed-ref, then resolve the record back out of git at the pinned blob and assert it equals the pre-compaction text byte-for-byte — an assertion FAILURE at the defect, not an error. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a DROP still keeps exactly one ledger line and no second copy (the lc-44 ruling is untouched — its reason was never in the body), and the conservation identity still balances with compacted > 0 for the first time.

So EXEMPT would spare 47 of 49 DONE bodies — every one that carries the mass — and leave compaction reaching only the 18 short DROPPED bodies: a verb that cannot touch what it exists to reduce. And LIFT-THE-TEXT only relocates the mass: closed-reason runs to a median 1067 chars and a max 3722, ~60k chars total, which would move out of the carrier and into a LEDGER read chronologically and growing unbounded. Both options carried a cost this desk regretted, which is the all-regrettable shape that owes a solved-before search before any round ships. ## lc-146
grade: DONE
requirement: cli.py's `_carrier_verb` (around lines 876-903) ends in an UNGUARDED FALLTHROUGH: `return verbs.cmd_item_close(args, out, ctx)`. It is safe today only because the call site's action tuple gates which actions reach it — so an action ADDED to that tuple without its own branch silently runs CLOSE, a destructive two-file MOVE, under another verb's name. The guard is a coincidence of the caller rather than a property of the function, and the next verb added to that carrier is the firing input. Record: opus-lc58-47 lane, reported not touched, 2026-09-15.
goal: lean-machinery-strict-checks
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,test/test_verbs.py
done-criterion: The fallthrough cannot run CLOSE for an unrecognised action: either every action dispatches from an explicit branch and an unknown one RAISES or returns could-not-verify, or the fallthrough names the single action it serves and refuses anything else. RED-FIRST, and it is constructible without touching the caller: drive _carrier_verb with an action string that is in no branch and assert it does NOT reach cmd_item_close — today it does, as an assertion FAILURE at the defect. MUST-NOT-MOVE: every action that legitimately dispatches today still reaches the same verb, asserted per action rather than in aggregate, since an aggregate arm passes on a refuse-everything build.
evidence: Found and reported rather than fixed by the lc-58/lc-47 lane 2026-09-15, whose own cli.py change adds a branch ABOVE that line and so would have masked it: 'safe today only because the call site's tuple gates it — an action added to that tuple without its own branch silently runs CLOSE'. The hazard is that the safety lives in the CALLER while the risk lives in the callee, so a future edit to the tuple carries a destructive default nobody reading the tuple would see.
blocked-by: NONE
blocker-moot: lc-58 (the blocker closed before this item did)
closed-reason: 2026-09-15 2026-09-15 `_carrier_verb` no longer ends in an unguarded `return verbs.cmd_item_close(...)`. `close` gets its own branch like every other action and anything else answers COULD NOT VERIFY, so an action added to the caller's tuple without a branch REFUSES instead of silently running a two-file MOVE under another verb's name. The fix uses the idiom the site already had — main's verb, lane, workflow and desk chains each end in an explicit refusal — so it introduces no concept the file did not carry. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK at 5b47f3b, in clones: battery run=614 failures=0 errors=0 skipped=1 read off the RESULT OBJECT; roster 88/88 CLEAN with both derivations agreeing; commit touches exactly its two paths. THE CROSS-LANE ANCHOR CHECK PASSED, run here as promised to the sibling that recorded the proof: both prove-rows arrangements anchored in cli.py are intact at this commit — [unregistered_kind] PROVEN and [migrate_repeated_from] PROVEN, each naming exactly its own row, 75 of 88, exit 0. The lane pre-answered it by reading its own diff's DELETION side before anything ran: exactly one removed line, its test file +188/-0 and purely appended. THE ARM CARRIES ITS OWN POSITIVE CONTROL AND THE LANE EXPLAINED WHY IT NEEDS ONE: the assertion is a NEGATIVE — an unbranched action never reaches the destructive close — and `_carrier_verb` returns before any dispatch when the context does not resolve, so 'no verb was called' would be true for free. The arm drives a legitimate action first and requires it to be SEEN reaching its verb. Without that, it would pass on a build where nothing dispatches at all. TWO MUTATIONS per the devbook, both assertion FAILURES with the mutation confirmed on disk first: the BUILD mutation reconstructs the real defect (an action added to the tuple with no branch) and the ARRANGEMENT mutation blinds the extraction, answering 'zero means the extraction stopped matching the source and every assertion below it is vacuous'. LAW 24 WAS USED AS A QUESTION RATHER THAN A RITUAL: the lane checked whether its change emits a new FINDING, found it does not, and concluded no registry row was owed and no boundary reach was needed. That is the check the desk keeps failing to make on its own briefs. RESIDUALS, all named by the lane rather than discovered: the two node arms were outside the battery and not run; the refusal path is unreachable END TO END through the real CLI surface by construction, since argparse choices cannot deliver an action the caller does not admit, so the arms drive the function directly and the docstring says why; prove-rows was never run in the shared copy. AND ONE RUN THE LANE REFUSED TO STAND BEHIND: an earlier live-tree battery printed failures=1 and the lane had piped it through `tail`, destroying the failure list. It graded that COULD NOT VERIFY rather than 'foreign red proven', gave the provenance it had (sibling files modified inside the window; the modified set grew between two status reads), and then downgraded its own attribution unprompted, pointing at this desk's integration run as the real evidence rather than its inference. Its own lesson is the transferable half: piping a battery through `tail` turns 'answer with the failure LIST' into a count.

## lc-148
grade: DONE
requirement: COMPACTION MAKES ID REUSE REACHABLE, and the allocator's own docstring names the premise the new verb breaks. next_ident allocates the lowest unused <prefix>-<n> and says why it reads both homes: 'EVERY home is read, live and closed. Ids are immutable across moves, so an id allocator that looked only at the live carrier would re-issue the id of everything ever closed — and the collision would surface as a DUPLICATE finding months later, in a file nobody was editing.' Compaction removes the body from EVERY home, so that premise no longer holds and the id is re-issued. Measured by the lc-58/lc-47 lane: after compacting xx-1, `item add` printed 'added xx-1 [READY] → ITEMS.md' while LEDGER.md already carried 'where the compacted body of xx-1 resolves'. `item check` reports CLEAN throughout — nothing fails, which is the whole problem. migrate.py:796-798 anticipated this in prose and nothing guards it.
