goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/retire.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: A compacted id is never re-issued. The allocator gains a reader of compacted ids — the ledger's compaction decision lines are the record the design already names, so the ids are there to be read — or compaction itself records the id somewhere next_ident consults. RED-FIRST on the measured walk: in a scratch repo, add an item, close it, compact it, then `item add` and assert the new id is NOT the compacted one; today it is, as an assertion FAILURE at the defect. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the allocator still re-uses nothing else and still refuses when no id-prefix is declared; and ids stay immutable across ordinary moves, which is the property next_ident's docstring is actually about. THE DESIGN QUESTION THIS ITEM DOES NOT PRE-EMPT, named so a lane does not settle it silently: whether a compacted id is permanently retired or whether the record's presence is enough to skip it. Both keep the invariant; they differ in whether the ledger must be parsed at allocation time, and that is the desk's call at brief time, not the lane's.
evidence: Measured by the executing lane in a scratch repo and confirmed at this desk BY MECHANISM rather than by re-running it: items.py next_ident's docstring states the invariant it depends on in its own words, and compaction is the first verb that removes a body from every home, so it invalidates exactly that premise. The lane judged it bookable rather than blocking because recoverability is unaffected — the BLOB is content-addressed and resolves unambiguously, only the ID collides — and it declined to fix it, correctly: the allocator would need a reader of compacted ids, a design the brief did not name, so building it would have been designing at the executing tier. NOTHING IS AT RISK TODAY: the 69 live done bodies in this repo have never been compacted and the verb has never been run on a real carrier, which is why this is booked rather than treated as an incident.

amend-reason: 2026-09-15 DESIGN RULED, and the ground is firmer than the one recommended to me. The judgment desk recommended skip-by-reading-the-record over a tombstone store and argued it from DRIFT — a separate store can diverge from the compaction record silently. True, and I take the recommendation, but the deciding ground is one rung down: a tombstone store is a SECOND HOME for a fact the ledger already holds, and invariant 3 forbids that outright. Drift is what makes a second home dangerous; the invariant is what makes it inadmissible, and an item citing the invariant cannot be re-litigated on someone's estimate of how likely drift is. I also checked for a third option the two-way framing might hide — leaving a stub id in the done home — and lc-58's own design refuses it: the body LEAVES the carrier, which is the point of the verb, so a stub that keeps the carrier from shrinking is the exempt branch under another name. The ruling reuses ledger.parse_line rather than adding a reader, which is this repo's fix-placement law: the change sits where the invariant is consumed and reuses what that site already computes.

Compaction is the first verb that removes a body from every home, so the fix is to make the COMPACTION RECORD a home next_ident reads: the ledger's compaction decision line already carries the folded ids, and ledger.parse_line already reads that line back, so this reuses the parser the site already has rather than introducing a concept. THE ALTERNATIVE IS REFUSED ON THIS REPO'S OWN INVARIANT, not on preference: a separate retired-id or tombstone store would be a SECOND HOME for a fact the ledger already holds, which invariant 3 forbids outright, and it would be a comparison basis restated from its source — free to drift from the compaction record with nothing failing. MUST-NOT-MOVE: ids stay immutable across ORDINARY moves (the property the docstring is actually about), the allocator still refuses when no id-prefix is declared, and a repo whose ledger has no compaction line allocates exactly as it does today — asserted, since a change that made every allocation consult a missing file would pass a reuse test while breaking every fresh repo.

Anything written in items.py alone would be dead code and the red walk would stay red — which is this item's own MUST-NOT-MOVE read backwards. The pattern is exact and it is the desk's: law 24 was minted from instances one and two and covers the REFUSAL surface; the desk then checks law 24, finds it satisfied, and treats the boundary as answered. Instance three needed the VERB and CHECKER surfaces; this one needs the ALLOCATOR's call sites. ## lc-120
grade: DONE
requirement: a closure REASON that turns out to be wrong has no repair path, so the done home keeps asserting it. item amend refuses a closed body and its refusal is RIGHT — 'the done home holds what was true when the item closed, and correcting it there would edit a record other counts already read' — but the consequence is that a superseded closure reason stands forever while its correction lives only in a ledger line the done-home reader never loads. That is the appended-correction failure at carrier scale: both versions stand and the reader who stops at the first takes the superseded one. Measured on lc-81, 2026-09-13, at this desk and by this desk's own error
goal: enforce-the-invariants

THE DESIGN IS RULED so it is not re-derived: APPEND ONLY. The original closed-reason and closed-ref are never rewritten, because the refusal's stated ground is correct — other counts have already read them — and a pointer is not an edit of the record, it is the record saying a later one exists. The verb REFUSES where the ledger ref does not resolve, so the pointer cannot dangle. RED-FIRST: lc-81's body in ITEMS-DONE.md today asserts a closure reason superseded the same hour by LEDGER decision 7113d78 and says nothing about it; assert the pointer is absent, then present, with the original closed-reason byte-identical across the change. amended-requirement: 2026-09-13 a closed body's slots cannot be corrected, so any that turn out FALSE keep asserting themselves forever — and the label they wear makes it worse. item amend refuses a closed body and its refusal is RIGHT ('the done home holds what was true when the item closed, and correcting it there would edit a record other counts already read'), but the consequence is that a superseded closure reason, done-criterion or EVIDENCE slot stands while its correction lives only in a ledger line the done-home reader never loads. That is the appended-correction failure at carrier scale: both versions stand and the reader who stops at the first takes the superseded one. The sharpest case is an evidence slot opening with VERIFIED AT THIS DESK over a claim nobody can reproduce, because the label is exactly what stops the next reader checking

## lc-160
grade: DROPPED
requirement: THE COMMISSIONED CRITIQUE PASS IS THE ONLY MECHANISM MEASURED TO CATCH A DESK'S OWN INSTRUMENT DEFECTS, AND IT FIRES FOR DISPATCHES ONLY. The dispatch skill requires every brief to ask, before the lane's first build call, which Background line is unopened or wrong and which two brief lines contradict each other, returned on the report channel and continuing without waiting for a reply. It is a FORM with a named output, so its absence is visible to the dispatcher rather than being a private reading. Nothing fires it anywhere else: not for a peer session receiving a claim, not for a desk's own load-bearing claims, not at the seam where a desk ships an instrument it built itself. The gap is that the party best placed to find the defect is the one who must RELY on the instrument, and outside a dispatch nothing obliges them to speak before acting.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: UNKNOWN. The mechanism home is itself the design question: a lifecycle verb, a plugin hook at a named seam, or a checked carrier convention; decided before this leaves PARKED
done-criterion: UNKNOWN, depends on the home decision recorded in the blocker
evidence: MEASURED 2026-09-18: three instrument defects in one dispatch, all found by sonnet lanes against an opus desk, all in the critique pass before any extraction ran, none catchable by the aggregate check the desk had already run. (1) The desk's claim that its extractor worked rested on matching totals (25 = 5 peer + 20 operator), equally consistent with compensating misclassifications; lane 3 named it, and the desk then measured 0 disagreements across 865 messages between the loose classifier and a strict one, so the hazard is real by construction and absent in fact. (2) THREE populations share the queue-operation record type, not two: measured 726 records = 291 empty + 206 peer + 168 task-notification + 61 REAL operator, so 73% of what the instrument called operator was background task notifications, inflating the ESCAPED bucket about 4x and hiding MECHANISM; found by lane 1. (3) STRUCTURAL: the instrument is keyed to INBOUND records while SELF and MECHANISM catches produce none, so it could see only two of the four buckets it was built to compare; found by lane 2. That one would have produced a CROSS-dominant tally matching the desk own prior expectation, from three lanes agreeing with each other while sharing one instrument, agreement certifying nothing on an axis none of them varied. grade: DONE

## lc-171
grade: DONE
requirement: DESK STATE IS WRITTEN BY A LIFECYCLE VERB AND GOVERNED BY NO KIND. $XDG_STATE_HOME/lifecycle/desk-state/ exists and is written by `lifecycle desk state`; no registered kind names its home. Exactly the gap lc-166 just closed for investigation records, one directory over, and found by the same survey. It has no home stage, no writer stage, no reader, no staleness, no exit and no growth control, while a shipped verb writes it — the plugin governing its own state everywhere except here.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: .claude/lifecycle.json,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,test/test_declaration.py
done-criterion: desk state resolves to a registered kind with all six stages, on the pattern lc-166 established and the fire log set before it: an XDG home declared as a per-repo kind, files never moved into any tree. The WRITER is verb:desk state, so its trigger is implicit in the act — which makes it one of the self-administering kinds and worth saying so in the declaration. RED-FIRST: with desk-state files present and the kind undeclared, the lc-166 check (records_kind_undeclared, or its sibling) must fire; declaring it takes the walk to 0 with the sweep still clean. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the verb ALWAYS OVERWRITES and keeps no history by design, so the staleness stage says the state is current-by-construction and the exit is not a compaction.
evidence: Measured at the artifact 2026-09-18: ~/.local/state/lifecycle/desk-state/ exists; a scan of the 24 declared kinds for a desk-state home returns none. desk.py own docstring states the design (one current state per desk, no history, XDG rather than .claude/ because a config-directory write costs a permission dialog). Found in the same survey that found lc-170, prompted by the build desk structural finding on lc-166: kind sweep walks tracked files while invariant 1 says every persisted thing, so XDG-homed kinds are invisible to it and their absence from the declaration is invisible to every other check too.

Where it would have failed: if lc-182 were undesigned, this stays open, because someone will build a detector eventually is not a home. ## lc-182
grade: DONE

The records check exists but is scoped by the repo name, which only works because a record filename carries its repo; desk state carries none, so the same check shape over it demands every repo declare a kind because some other repo session wrote a file — measured, 22 roster rows could-not-verify in one run, law 11 firing on legitimate work.

RED-FIRST: a repo with its own desk-state file and no declaration fires; a repo with ANOTHER repo desk-state file present and none of its own stays clean, and that second arm is the one the withdrawn first cut failed. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the verb still ALWAYS OVERWRITES one file per desk and keeps no history, so the repo field is an added key rather than a second file or a history.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk 2026-09-18 while building lc-171: the first cut of the check took the roster from CLEAN to 22 rows COULD NOT VERIFY in one run, because every declaration row runs kind check over a scratch repo while the check read the machine home. MEASURED at the artifact: a desk-state file holds value, argument, at, desk, desk_source — no repo, which is why the records scoping does not transfer. DERIVED: two undeclared XDG kinds found by hand in one day is the rate that makes a detector worth its own item, and the same blindness will hide the third. RELAYED from the judgment desk, carried as theirs: this class is lc-178's shape, a demand at a door contaminating every control that passes through it.

RED-FIRST, both arms: a repo that wrote desk state and declares no kind FIRES; a repo with ANOTHER repo's file present and none of its own stays CLEAN, and that second arm is precisely the one lc-171's withdrawn cut failed. Row desk_state_kind_undeclared PROVEN at its real anchor and FAILED at an inert one. MUST-NOT-MOVE HELD: the verb still always overwrites one file per desk and keeps no history — repo is an added key, not a second file or a history. A FILE WITHOUT THE KEY BELONGS TO NOBODY by design: state written before this cannot be attributed, and guessing would re-create the over-fire one layer in. ONE FINDING ABOUT MY OWN PROOF, recorded because it is the same class: the first recorded arrangement mutated the repo SCOPE and prove-rows answered rows changed NONE — correctly, since removing a scope makes a check fire MORE and the plant still fires. A row's pair proves the refusal's axis; reach is proven by the arm that must stay silent. The withdrawn check had a passing red-first arm and no reach arm at all.

## lc-183
grade: DONE
requirement: 82% OF THE MACHINE-WIDE FIRE LOG IS TEST POLLUTION, AND COMPACTION WOULD TREAT THE SYMPTOM. Measured 2026-09-18 at the artifact: 1,211,939 records / 132MB, of which 989,245 (81.6%) carry a repo path under /tmp — scratch repos built by this suite and by prove-rows. Top verbs are item add (221,952), migrate (194,236), item statusline (144,993), item check (129,516). The busiest single day is 2026-09-15 at 435,065 records, which is a test-run signature and not a day of work. TWO TEST FILES ISOLATE XDG_STATE_HOME (test_desk.py, test_declaration.py) AND THE REST OF THE SUITE DOES NOT, so every other arm — and every prove-rows walk, which runs the whole roster once per recorded arrangement — appends to the OPERATOR'S REAL machine-wide log. This is the corpus's partial-override class exactly: a fixture that isolates the destination while a sibling global stays keyed to live state aims the exercised path at real data outside the test, and its green is identical. The exercised path here is an APPEND, which the corpus names as one of the three destructive members.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: test/,tools/prove-rows.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/firelog.py
done-criterion: no test run and no prove-rows walk writes to the real machine-wide fire log. THE ISOLATION SET IS CHECKED AGAINST THE GLOBALS THE EXERCISED PATH CONSUMES, never against the ones a given test happens to touch — which is the rule the current two-file isolation violates. RED-FIRST, and it is cheap and exact: record the real log's line count, run the full suite plus a prove-rows walk, and the count MUST NOT MOVE; today that same probe moves it by thousands. MUST-NOT-MOVE (1): the fire log keeps recording REAL verb runs — the fix isolates tests, it does not silence the instrument, and a fix that stopped the log recording would trade a polluted register for no register. MUST-NOT-MOVE (2): the two files that already isolate keep doing so; this widens the isolation rather than relocating it. NOTE FOR lc-181: this does not replace compaction, it removes the reason most of the volume exists. Compaction over a log that is 82% test noise compacts noise.
evidence: MEASURED at the artifact 2026-09-18, parsed rather than estimated: 1,211,939 records, 132MB, 989,245 under /tmp (81.6%), 74,379 with an empty repo field, first record 2026-08-26T10:51:44Z (verb 'kind check', a scratch repo), last 2026-09-18T16:30:48Z. Verb and day breakdowns as stated in the requirement. MEASURED, with a positive control so the absence claim holds: grep for XDG_STATE_HOME across test/ and tools/ returns hits ONLY in test_desk.py, test_declaration.py and two prose mentions, while the same grep over the package finds firelog.py:38 — so the instrument matches and the isolation is genuinely partial rather than merely unfound. DERIVED: firelog.state_dir() reads XDG_STATE_HOME or falls back to ~/.local/state, so an un-isolated test inherits the operator's real home by construction. RELATION TO lc-170, which is why this was invisible: the growth walk could not resolve the home at all until today, so nothing examined this population — the alarm that would have flagged the growth and the pollution that caused it were hidden by the same defect.
blocked-by: NONE
closed-reason: 2026-09-18 2026-09-18 the suite and prove-rows write to a scratch state home; the operator's machine-wide fire log stops receiving test records. RED-FIRST, the desk's own cheap probe, run on both sides against the real file: one suite run appended 869 records before, 0 after; prove-rows 183 before, 0 after. MUST-NOT-MOVE HELD AND ASSERTED: the log still records real verb runs — an arm writes through firelog.fire and reads it back, because an isolation that worked by breaking the logger would trade a polluted register for no register — and the two files that already isolated keep doing so, now restoring to the run's scratch value rather than the machine's. TWO ENTRY POINTS, because the isolation set is checked against what the exercised path CONSUMES rather than what one entry point imports: prove-rows spawns children that import lifecycle_core directly and never touch test/. ONE MEASURED SURPRISE: test/__init__.py is the obvious single place and is DEAD under this repo's own declared command — unittest discover -s test imports modules top-level and never imports the package. Probed both forms (0 imports vs 1) rather than assuming; a fix resting on that premise would have been a green that changed nothing, which is the defect's own shape. So the import is per-module with a check DERIVING the module list from the directory, and a known-positive arm proving that detector can fire. Side effect worth recording: the suite now runs in half the time, 42s to 21s, which is what appending to a 132MB file was costing every run.

## lc-205
grade: DONE
requirement: THE SCHEMA APPLY WRITES WITH A DIFFERENT PREDICATE THAN THE DRY RUN READS WITH, AND A MISS PRINTS written: AND EXITS CLEAN. Two predicates over one contract: the PLAN reads a carrier head through declaration.carrier_schema, which partitions on the first colon and compares head.strip() == 'schema' (declaration.py:1566-1567); the WRITE matches raw.strip().startswith('schema:') (migrate.py:2003). The write loop has NO else branch, so a head the reader accepts and the writer does not is put back unchanged while the written: line prints anyway. A head spelled with a space before the colon falsifies it. THIS IS THE BLAST RADIUS THAT MAKES lc-192 OWED BEFORE lc-168: law 25 makes the dry run the thing that licenses applying across every declared repo, and this class is invisible to a dry run BY CONSTRUCTION because the dry run exercises the READER's predicate and never the WRITER's.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_migrate.py
done-criterion: ONE body decides what a schema head is, and both the plan and the write resolve through it — the lane list / item ready lesson this repo's laws file already states for that pair. A head the reader accepts and the writer cannot rewrite is a FINDING or COULD NOT VERIFY, never a written: line at exit 0. MUST-NOT-BUILD: the repair is not widening the writer's startswith to also accept the spaced form — that leaves two predicates that merely agree today. RED-FIRST, arrangement recorded: a carrier head spelled with a space before the colon, declaration and carrier both at 1, migrate --schema-from 1 --apply. Before: prints written and APPLIED at exit 0, carrier md5 UNCHANGED, declaration advanced. After: the run does not report a write it did not make. CONTROL that must stay green: the ordinary head bumps and rewrites exactly as now.
evidence: MEASURED at this desk, reproduced rather than taken on the lane's report — two scratch repos, all three XDG roots redirected, control first, exit codes read WITHOUT a pipe. MEASURED control, head 'schema: 1': written: ITEMS.md (schema 1 -> 2), APPLIED, exit 0, head after 'schema: 2', carrier rewritten YES, declaration 2. MEASURED probe, head 'schema : 1', everything else identical: the SAME written: ITEMS.md (schema 1 -> 2) and APPLIED at exit 0, head after still 'schema : 1', carrier rewritten NO, declaration 2. MEASURED deadlock: kind check then reports FINDING schema_mismatch naming declaration 2 against carrier 1 and prints the repair 'lifecycle migrate --schema-from 1'; that command REFUSES at exit 3 — the flag is the caller's claim about where this repo is and a mismatch must not be silently resolved. So the printed repair cannot run and the only remaining exit is hand-editing a carrier law 8 makes the tool the sole writer of. RELAYED from the lc-192 lane and reproduced here rather than carried: the two predicate line numbers and the absent else branch. DERIVED, not executed: that no other caller resolves a schema head through a third predicate — the sweep this item also owes. MEASURED divergence from the lane, and by law 17 a claim about my instrument first: the lane measured kind check at exit 2, I measure 3, because my minimal fixture carries an extra could-not-verify check; the finding itself reproduced identically.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 Three additions from lifecycle-6f's independent reproduction, each verified at this desk rather than carried: the witness class is any INTERNAL whitespace including a tab and not just a space, with the leading-space case as the control that pins where the divergence lives; a THIRD predicate exists — item check's shape checker already rejects this head, which places the fix on an existing parser instead of a new one; and the blast radius is bounded by the pre-commit hook to working trees and hookless repos, which is narrower than the first booking implied and still crosses lc-168's path. Also records that the lane/desk exit-code divergence is a fixture difference explained by exits.worst(), not an instrument error on either side.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-18 ONE body decides what a schema head is, and every reader resolves through it. THE FIX IS PLACED, NOT INVENTED: the repo ALREADY OWNS a predicate that rejects this head — item check's shape checker calls it out — and migrate simply does not ask it. The repair is to ask the existing one at the site where the invariant is CONSUMED, never to add a fourth parser. MUST-NOT-BUILD, AND THE WITNESS CLASS IS WHY: the repair is not widening the writer's startswith to also accept a space. ANY whitespace between 'schema' and the colon splits the two predicates — a TAB does it too — while LEADING whitespace does not, because both sides strip. A fix that special-cases the space is visibly insufficient and the tab arm is what proves it. A head the reader accepts and the writer cannot rewrite is a FINDING or COULD NOT VERIFY, never a written: line at exit 0. RED-FIRST, arrangement recorded: declaration and carrier both at 1, head spelled with an internal space, migrate --schema-from 1 --apply. Before: prints written and APPLIED at exit 0 with the carrier's CONTENT unchanged. After: the run does not report a write it did not make. THREE ARMS, not one: internal space, internal tab, and the LEADING-space control which must stay GREEN — that control is what proves the fix targets the divergence rather than blanket-rejecting whitespace.

Control 'schema: 1': written: ITEMS.md (schema 1 -> 2), APPLIED, exit 0, head after 'schema: 2', md5 CHANGED, declaration 2. Probe 'schema : 1': the SAME written: and APPLIED at exit 0, head after 'schema : 1', md5 UNCHANGED, declaration 2. MEASURED third predicate, run at this desk: item check on that head reports FINDING [item_shape] in two voices — 'head line 1 is not key: value' AND 'the file carries no schema: <n> head line' — so the shape checker says the line is ABSENT while carrier_schema reads it as 1 and returns a number. THREE readers of one line, pairwise disagreeing, and migrate pairs the two that disagree silently. MEASURED deadlock: kind check names the mismatch and prints the repair 'migrate --schema-from 1', which REFUSES at exit 3. BLAST RADIUS BOUNDED HONESTLY, relayed from lifecycle-6f and consistent with the item_shape finding I ran: the bad head cannot be COMMITTED through the pre-commit hook, so the reachable population is a working tree, a repo without the hook installed, or a carrier written before it existed — narrower than 'any declared repo', not zero, and lc-168 crosses repos whose hook state nobody has read. EXIT-CODE DIVERGENCE RESOLVED, and it was never a disagreement: the lc-192 lane measured kind check at 2 and I measured 3 because exits.worst() puts could-not-verify ABOVE finding and my minimal fixture carries an extra could-not-verify check. Both instruments read true over different check populations — a shared-coordinate difference. 2 belongs to the lane's fixture, 3 to mine.
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 ESCALATION, measured at this desk while reading the two sites to design the fix. The defect is not only that the apply reports a write it did not make — the writer, having failed to match the head, CONTINUES SCANNING AND REWRITES A BODY LINE, silently corrupting content while printing a clean carrier bump. That is a data-corruption path on the apply branch that law 25's dry run licenses across every declared repo. It also changes the fix: the two predicates must agree on WHICH LINE is the head and not merely on its spelling, so the writer consumes the reader's position rather than re-scanning. Reachability measured and bounded in the same pass: not reachable in this repo's current carriers, reachable in the archive region, a ledger body, or another repo's carriers.
amended-requirement: 2026-09-18 THE SCHEMA APPLY AND THE DRY RUN USE DIFFERENT PREDICATES, AND THE APPLY CAN REWRITE THE WRONG LINE — A SILENT BODY CORRUPTION, NOT MERELY A MISSED WRITE. Two predicates over one contract, and they disagree on BOTH halves. WHAT a head is: the PLAN uses declaration.carrier_schema (skip comments and blanks, partition on the first colon, head.strip() == 'schema'); the WRITE uses raw.strip().startswith('schema:') (migrate.py:2003). WHICH LINE is the head: the reader inspects only the FIRST non-comment line and gives up there; the writer scans EVERY line and rewrites the first match. Those coincide only while the head is spelled the writer's way. When it is not, the writer does not stop — it keeps scanning into the BODY and rewrites the first line below that happens to start with 'schema:', leaving the real version line untouched, and prints written: and APPLIED at exit 0. Law 25 makes the dry run the thing that licenses applying across every declared repo, and this class is invisible to a dry run BY CONSTRUCTION because the dry run exercises the READER and never the WRITER.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-18 ONE body decides BOTH what a schema head is AND which line carries it, and the write rewrites the line the READER identified rather than re-scanning for its own match. That is the shape of the fix: carrier_schema already walks to the head and knows its index — the writer consumes that, so the two cannot disagree about spelling or position by construction. THE FIX IS PLACED, NOT INVENTED: the repo already owns the deciding predicate and migrate simply does not ask it. MUST-NOT-BUILD: not a widened startswith, and not a fourth parser. Widening the writer to accept a space leaves two predicates that merely agree today and does NOTHING about the wrong-line half. RED-FIRST, FOUR ARMS: (1) internal space — the carrier's content must not come back unchanged under a written: line; (2) internal TAB — same, and it is what proves the fix is not a space special-case; (3) THE CORRUPTION ARM, and it is the one that must go red loudest — a head in the reader-only spelling with a body line below whose stripped form starts with 'schema:' must NOT have that body line rewritten; (4) CONTROL THAT MUST STAY GREEN — a head with LEADING whitespace still migrates normally, because both predicates strip leading space and an over-broad fix that refuses all whitespace breaks the good case.

The migration wrote into an item body and reported a clean carrier bump. MEASURED predicate matrix: 'schema: 1' -> reader (1,None) / writer True agree; 'schema : 1' -> (1,None)/False DIVERGE; 'schema<TAB>: 1' -> (1,None)/False DIVERGE; '  schema: 1' -> (1,None)/True agree. REACHABILITY MEASURED HONESTLY AND IT BOUNDS THE CORRUPTION ARM: this repo's three live carriers hold EXACTLY ONE schema-prefixed line each — ITEMS.md line 1, ITEMS-DONE.md line 6, LEDGER.md line 21 — so the corruption is NOT reachable in this repo today. The population is any carrier carrying a second line whose stripped form begins with 'schema:' below the head: the done home's archive region, which is held verbatim and ungraded; a ledger body; or another declared repo's carriers, whose content nobody has read. Not zero, and lc-168 crosses exactly those. BLAST RADIUS, relayed from lifecycle-6f and consistent with the item_shape finding I ran: the bad head cannot be COMMITTED through the pre-commit hook, so the reachable population is working trees, hookless repos, and carriers written before the hook existed. MEASURED third predicate: item check on that head reports FINDING [item_shape] in two voices — 'head line 1 is not key: value' AND 'the file carries no schema: <n> head line' — so the shape checker says the line is ABSENT while carrier_schema returns 1. THREE readers of one line, pairwise disagreeing, and migrate pairs the two that disagree silently. EXIT-CODE DIVERGENCE RESOLVED, not a disagreement: the lane measured kind check at 2 and this desk at 3 because exits.worst() puts could-not-verify above finding and the minimal fixture carries an extra could-not-verify check; 2 belongs to the lane's fixture, 3 to mine.

## lc-168
grade: DONE
requirement: A KIND DECLARES WHAT, WHERE, WHO, FOR WHOM AND HOW LONG — AND NOT WHEN. Checked at the schema: home, writer, reader, staleness, exit, growth. The missing stage is the TRIGGER, and it is exactly what separates a self-administering kind from one that needs a person. For the 4 verb-written kinds the trigger is IMPLICIT — item close writes a closure because the verb ran, so nobody remembers anything. For the 17 writer:session kinds there is no WHEN at all, which is why the operator decides it is time. The writer field was first read as predicting DRIFT; the sharper reading is that it predicts SELF-ADMINISTRATION and drift is the symptom. Operator, 2026-09-18: self-administering means the system decides where, when and what, not a person deciding each time.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_declaration.py
done-criterion: UNKNOWN pending the decision below, but the shape is known and the MECHANISM ALREADY EXISTS: lanes.evaluate_trigger runs a predicate and maps its exit to fire/quiet/broken, total on purpose so nothing falls into silence, and it is already the single evaluator shared by lane triggers and item ready evidence blockers. A seventh stage is that proven evaluator applied to a question the schema does not ask. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a kind whose trigger is genuinely implicit in a verb declares that rather than inventing a predicate, and an undeclared trigger is a FINDING the way an undeclared stage already is.
evidence: Measured at the schema 2026-09-18: a kind declares exactly home, writer, reader, staleness, exit, growth. Grouped by writer across 22 kinds: 4 verb-written (items, done bodies, ledger lines, the fire log) against 17 writer:session and 1 producer. The verb-written set is the self-administering set. Design of record docs/answerable-not-felt.md section 2b. Counter-case that makes it sharp: wan2gp carries a large heavily-READ knowledge layer (608KB backlog, 553-line CLAUDE.md, 57 docs) at about 4 operator interjections per 100 turns, and it is ad-hoc on where, when and what — knowledge without a system, working because the operator was the writer-of-last-resort.
blocked-by: NONE
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 DECIDED AT THIS DESK: a seventh DECLARED stage, not a property derived from the writer field. The derivation alternative cannot express the thing that is missing — deriving the trigger from the writer works only where the writer IS a verb, and that is exactly the 4 kinds that already self-administer. For the 17 writer:session kinds there is no verb to derive from, and those are the entire population the stage exists to fix. So derivation is well-defined precisely where it is not needed and undefined precisely where it is. The cost is real and accepted: this is a schema change, so law 25 makes it a migration dry-run over every declared repo, and the migrate verb already carries that machinery.
amended-done-criterion: 2026-09-18 a kind declares a SEVENTH stage, the TRIGGER: when the write fires. Values are either a verb (the trigger is implicit in the act, which is what the 4 self-administering kinds already have) or a predicate evaluated by lanes.evaluate_trigger, the single existing evaluator that maps exit to fire/quiet/broken totally so nothing falls into silence. An UNDECLARED trigger is a FINDING the way an undeclared stage already is. SHIPS ITS MIGRATION per law 25: dry-run first, over every declared repo, before it is applied anywhere. RED-FIRST: a declaration missing the stage is refused; a kind whose trigger names a verb that does not exist is refused; a predicate trigger that cannot run is COULD NOT VERIFY, never quiet. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a kind whose trigger genuinely IS a verb declares that rather than inventing a predicate.

Also records the two preconditions they discharged themselves rather than assigning: dotfiles' three carriers hold exactly one correctly-spelled schema line each, at the head, archive region included — so lc-205's corruption population is EMPTY in the target — and dotfiles' tree is clean with nothing unpushed, so nobody is standing in it.

ADDED 2026-09-18, NOT OPTIONAL, AND IT IS THE GENERAL FIX RATHER THAN lc-205'S CLASS: the apply READS BACK from the artifact and asserts the head EQUALS the new schema, PER CARRIER, before it reports APPLIED — never from the printed written: line, which is exactly what lied three times tonight in a run that changed nothing. The writing command's exit says the write happened, never that what arrived is what was meant; a read-back catches the missed write AND the body corruption AND anything in that family without knowing the class in advance. A carrier whose read-back disagrees is a FINDING or COULD NOT VERIFY and the run does not report APPLIED. If migrate turns out to have NO read-back on any of its other write paths, that is its own item and is booked rather than absorbed here.
amend-reason: 2026-09-18 Operator ruling 2026-09-18, relayed first-hand by lifecycle-6f: after a migration only the new state exists, legacy files are not left lying around, and git holds every prior state so retiring destroys nothing. Its reach into lc-168 is ONE STATED ABSENCE and no work, and I verified that rather than accepting it: the schema path reads no source carrier, confirmed two ways. The criterion therefore gains the requirement that a schema migration SAYS it retires nothing BECAUSE it read no source — so the absence is a stated answer and not a silence, which is the same rule as the read-back one altitude out. The ruling's real weight lands on the carrier-migration path and is booked separately rather than absorbed here.

Design of record docs/answerable-not-felt.md section 2b. Counter-case that makes it sharp: wan2gp carries a large heavily-READ knowledge layer (608KB backlog, 553-line CLAUDE.md, 57 docs) at about 4 operator interjections per 100 turns, and it is ad-hoc on where, when and what — knowledge without a system, working because the operator was the writer-of-last-resort. MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-18, confirming lifecycle-6f's reading of the operator's only-the-new-state-exists ruling, and confirmed TWO WAYS rather than one: (i) run() routes --schema-from before any source work and REFUSES --retire-source there with FINDING [retire_source_not_writing], whose own text says 'it reads no old carrier, so there is no source to retire and nothing this run writes supersedes one'; (ii) run_schema, migrate.py:1905-2032, mentions from_carrier, src_name, dispose and retire_source ZERO times across its 127 lines. So the schema path genuinely has no source and the ruling has nothing to delete here — the criterion gains a stated absence, not work. RELAYED from lifecycle-6f, executed by them at the artifact and to be RE-RUN by me at apply time because it is a state claim licensing an act: dotfiles' three declared carriers each hold exactly one schema-prefixed line, correctly spelled, at the head, archive region included — ITEMS.md:1, ITEMS-DONE.md:6, LEDGER.md:1, all 'schema: 2' — so lc-205's corruption population is EMPTY in the target; and dotfiles' tree is clean with nothing unpushed.

Same defect class this arc keeps finding, at the size of one slot.

blocker-moot: lc-205 (the blocker closed before this item did)
closed-reason: 2026-09-18 THE SEVENTH STAGE SHIPS, THIS REPO IS MIGRATED, AND THE HONEST NUMBER IS 80% none. Distribution over 25 kinds: verb 4, predicate 1, none-with-a-reason 20. Reported rather than tuned, on the axis the judgment desk sharpened: none-with-an-EVENT 20, none-with-only-an-ABSENCE 0 — two began as empty nones and were rewritten to name their firing moment. THE SENTENCE THAT KEEPS THIS FROM BEING READ AS CEREMONY: the stage MECHANIZES the kinds a verb writes and CONVERTS the rest from an unstated moment into a stated one, and a named unmechanizable event is strictly more than silence — the observer column filled in prose, which is what law 26 asks for and all it asks for. GRADING CAVEAT CARRIED, not buried: my bucketing was a substring check over phrases I chose, so the zero is my reading; lifecycle-6f spot-read 8 of the 20 independently and found no empty none, labelled by them as a SPOT CHECK and not a census. REACH FINDING, and it is the one worth keeping: the 20 kinds no verb WRITES are very nearly the 20 no verb READS — the same population — so this system is mechanized for its own carriers and declarative for everything else, which explains both this 80% and the read-timing gap mapped in docs/the-loop.md. VOCABULARY GAP RECORDED rather than papered over: the fire log declares none because EVERY verb writes it, so its trigger is the verb LAYER, which the vocabulary cannot spell; one arbitrary verb would have been the tidier lie. THE READ-BACK (law 25) SHIPPED AND FAILED ON ITS OWN SUBJECT FIRST: the plan derived two triggers, the apply wrote neither, the run printed APPLIED over a declaration that gained none — and the read-back passed it, because it checked the schema number, which was already correct and never in question. An instrument that re-opens the artifact and asks the wrong question returns exactly what a sound one returns. Found by hand-reading the file, by nothing in the run; both halves repaired, and the read-back now verifies every planned change rather than the version. Its own red-first ran first: lc-205 reintroduced WHOLE, the read-back catching the written: lie and naming the one bad target while the other three read back clean — and MY FIRST PROOF OF IT WAS WRONG, reintroducing the defect but keeping the new guard so the guard caught it before the read-back ran, which is the staged-control rule with my hand on it. THE FAILING TEST WAS REPAIRED WITH ITS REASON, not silenced: it anchored to a TOTAL FINDING COUNT over a 2026-08 tree, a live quantity every future refusal moves, so it was decaying into a false alarm from the day it was written and the seventh stage is merely the first refusal that moved it. The repair names the guard own ident and asserts the pair finding sets differ in EXACTLY that one — strictly stronger than the count, and unable to hide a guard that stopped firing, which the count could. That test sat in this item write-set by the dependents rule and nobody found it until it went red: the rule working, and the defect was the write-set that did not carry it. NOT DONE HERE, booked instead: SCHEMA_FLOOR stays 2 and the bump is OWED — lc-218, with its four gates named, three of them met at this commit. DOTFILES IS UNTOUCHED and its four kinds are the judgment desk to author. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK: suite 752 pass 0 fail 0 skip; roster 112 rows 112 passed 0 could-not-verify, including the two new rows trigger_verb_unknown and migration_readback_disagrees; kind check CLEAN at 25 kinds every stage declared; conservation clean.

## lc-219
grade: DONE
requirement: TWENTY OF TWENTY-FIVE KINDS DECLARE reader:session AND NO COMMAND FIRES THOSE READS. The session-start injection fires ONCE, before the session knows what it will need, and nothing fires at the moment of APPLICATION — which is where retrieval actually fails. Demonstrated 2026-09-18 by the judgment desk itself, which dispatched four research lanes past a document it had already handled 17 times. Operator decision, same day: the REGISTRY goes in front of every session — not the files, the MAP. A session holding the map knows a kind exists and where it lives, which is the precondition for reaching it at all. Same population as lc-168 measured from the other side: the 20 kinds no verb WRITES are very nearly the 20 no verb READS.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/cli.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,test/test_schema.py
done-criterion: kind list gains a --digest FLAG, not a new verb: the verb already exists and already reads the declaration, so law 24 four realizing surfaces are not owed and the existing reader is reused rather than a second one built. One line per kind: name, home, member count, newest member, and a session-read marker on kinds whose reader names session. COUNTING RULE, fixed here so nobody builds a second parser: for a GLOB or DIRECTORY home count matching files and name the newest by mtime; for a SINGLE-FILE home no count, just that file mtime date. DO NOT parse per-kind entry structure — journal entries, ledger lines, item blocks — that is a second body for what the owning verbs already read, and the digest is a POINTER SURFACE, never an authority on content. THE THIRD ANSWER IS REQUIRED AND IS THE EASIEST THING TO GET WRONG: a home that does not resolve renders COULD NOT VERIFY with its reason, NEVER zero files — a zero and an unreadable home are the same string to a reader, and the block is trusted at a glance. Reuse home_unresolvable; no new row. RED-FIRST, three arms: plant a file in a glob home and both the count and the newest name move; point a home at a path that does not exist and the assertion is on what must NOT appear, the zero-files string being absent; and a kind whose reader does not name session renders WITHOUT the marker — the control that proves the marker discriminates rather than decorating every line.
evidence: RELAYED from lifecycle-6f and carried as theirs: the 17-times incident, the operator decision, the counting rule and the three arms are all their text, and the site check is theirs too — kind list already exists and already renders the full stages per kind, so this is a flag on the wall form. ## lc-157
grade: DONE
requirement: PARKED 2026-09-18 — MAKE AGENT SELF-STATE ANSWERABLE, NOT FELT. A recurring, high-cost class this session (>=9 instances in one session): an agent asserts a STATE OF ITS OWN WORK from memory/feeling instead of querying it, and the wrong answer is shaped exactly like the right one (a clean green, a "nothing owed", a false absence) — so it is caught by a person, a peer, or an accident, never by the mechanism that should. The corpus NAMES the class (Fixing: one's own past output checked before asserted; the COUNT and the NEGATIVE are the under-served halves); what is missing is a MECHANISM that makes the felt state answerable at assertion time. requirement: migration_ledger_nonzero READS ONE OF ledger.parse's THREE ANSWERS AND PRINTS IT AS THE WHOLE. The check counts len(lparsed.lines) — readable, classifiable lines only — while ledger.parse splits its input THREE ways (lines, unreadable, problems) and its own docstring says so: 'A line this build cannot classify is UNREADABLE and counted apart — the same three answers'. So an unclassifiable ledger line renders as 'ledger lines: 0' and the run exits CLEAN. grade: DONE
requirement: A PROSE CLAIM BESIDE A REGISTERED VERIFY COMMAND IS HELD BY NOTHING, AND IT INHERITS THE MECHANISM'S AUTHORITY. lifecycle verify (e7c4a9a) parses the laws file's ## Verify fenced block and EXECUTES its commands, asserting EXECUTED against REGISTERED. It holds no expectation about the prose beside the fence. Measured 2026-09-18: this repo's own ## Verify stated that one of 51 node bites 'structurally cannot pass in this repo' and told the reader to expect the red; 7fe9e68 had repaired that in August and the file never moved with it, so for about a month the laws file trained its readers to discount a red at the one bite that exists to catch a silent scope collapse. The verb ran correctly, reported correctly, and the false sentence sat two lines above its output. Law 26 and J27.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verify.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_verify.py
done-criterion: an EXPECTATION about a registered verify command is declared in a form the verb CHECKS — an expected pass/fail count, or a named known-failure the verb asserts is STILL failing — so a bite that starts passing makes the declared expectation wrong and verify says so. THE DIRECTION THAT MATTERS IS THE SILENT ONE: a declared failure that starts passing must go RED, because that is the case that degrades the instrument rather than breaking it. RED-FIRST, and the firing input is the measured incident itself: declare one expected failure over this repo's node bites, run the block, get 62 pass / 0 fail, and the verb must report the declared expectation as WRONG rather than reporting the suite green. MUST-NOT-MOVE: a command with NO declared expectation stays legal and reports as today (executed/registered, no expectation graded) — the demand is that a STATED expectation be checkable, never that every command carry one; and whether an expectation is the RIGHT one stays judgment, since a guard over that fires on legitimate work (law 11).
evidence: MEASURED at the artifact 2026-09-18 by a peer desk running the block: 62 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped, the named bite among the passes; corrected in place at f104ee0. The repair that made the prose stale is 7fe9e68 (August), which derived the guard's roots from git ls-files. DERIVED from reading verify.py at this desk: parse_block extracts the fenced block's COMMANDS and the module's own docstring states its boundary as proving commands RAN and what they returned — the prose beside the fence is outside that predicate by construction, not by oversight. CLASS EVIDENCE, J27: this is the fourth of four instances in one day of the stack holding a thing that was not retrieved at the moment it applied, and the only one of the four that had a month to do damage, because the other three were caught inside the session that made them. THE ASYMMETRY that makes it worth a mechanism rather than a convention: an expectation going stale toward FAILING is loud and somebody looks; toward PASSING it is silent and it trains readers to pre-discount the one real red.

amend-reason: 2026-09-19 2026-09-19 pickup re-grounding at the executing desk. Two supersessions in one amendment. FIRST, the booked counts had drifted by one in both figures because a kind was registered after booking; recorded rather than silently rebuilt on, since the entry's numbers are what a later reader would check. SECOND and load-bearing, the entry framed its own first decision as sub-field-versus-eighth-stage decided on COST, and the artifact says the discriminator is ARITY: 13 of 26 kinds have mixed reader lists that a per-kind scalar cannot describe. That reframing is what makes the decision answerable at the declaration instead of by taste, and it is the mechanism-5 act the directive asks for at pickup.

THE MEASUREMENT THE ENTRY DID NOT HAVE, and it decides the item's first question: read moments are PER READER, not per kind. 17 of 26 kinds carry more than one reader entry, and 13 of 26 are MIXED, a session reader beside a verb or hook reader. Worked case: kind laws reads [session, verb:audit], where the verb reader's moment is implicit in the verb running and the session reader's moment is the O6 gap itself. An eighth stage is a per-KIND scalar and can name only ONE moment, so it cannot describe half this declaration and is the wrong SHAPE for the fact rather than merely the dearer branch. DERIVED, at the artifact: the third option, riding the WHEN inside the reader token string, is dead. declaration.parse_refs splits on commas and accepts only bare session/operator or lane|verb|hook|producer:<name>; anything else is refused as reference_untyped PROSE, so a when: suffix inside the string cannot be expressed. Decision recorded in LEDGER at 8a5d760. CONSEQUENCE CARRIED RATHER THAN BURIED: the reader entry gains an object form beside today's string, so schema 3 stops identifying the shape, this owes its OWN numbered bump per lc-218 MUST-NOT-BUILD, law 25 binds it to a dry-run over BOTH declared repos, and the dotfiles apply is outside this desk's write boundary and is a boundary halt to the driving desk on the lc-218 pattern. UNVERIFIED and NOT claimed: no migration has been dry-run yet and no line of the implementation is written; the bump's necessity is argued from lc-218's own stated criterion that the version must identify the shape, not from an executed check.
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 2026-09-19 SELF-CORRECTION, same session, one hour after the previous amendment: migrate.py is REMOVED again, having been added on caution rather than on a read. EXECUTED since: SCHEMA_FLOOR is single-source at declaration.py:63, with ledger.py:45 and items.py:57 taking it by assignment from decl, so a floor bump is ONE line and leaves nothing silently behind (lc-218's done-criterion states this property and the artifact confirms it). migrate.py derives its target from decl.SCHEMA_FLOOR at 1975 and items_mod.SCHEMA_FLOOR at 2561/2633/2640 with no hardcoded version anywhere, so migrate --schema-from is generic over the bump. And a grep for reader and KIND_STAGES across migrate.py returns no handling of the declaration's reader STAGE at all, only the English word, so the additive object form gives it nothing to transform. test/test_schema.py STAYS, on lc-218's precedent, because the single-source floor test is what proves the bump did not leave a module behind. WHY THIS IS RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED: a write-set is the join's input and an invented path collides with nothing by construction, so the join's worst misread presents as its cleanest answer. An over-broad write-set is the mirror defect, serializing lanes that need not serialize, and it would have made this item collide with lc-175 and lc-179 on migrate.py for no reason.

closed-reason: 2026-09-19 2026-09-19 THE READER STAGE HAS A WHEN, AND THE NUMBER THAT IDENTIFIES THE SHAPE MOVED WITH IT. Two acts, deliberately not one, per lc-218's MUST-NOT-BUILD: 9df084b shipped the shape, 2e533f0 the floor. THE FIRST DECISION WAS DECIDED ON ARITY, NOT COST, and that reframing is the item's real product: the entry and the directive both posed sub-field versus eighth stage as a price question, while the declaration says 17 of 26 kinds carry more than one reader and 13 are MIXED, a session reader beside a verb or hook reader. Kind laws reads [session, verb:audit]: the verb reader's moment is implicit in the verb running, the session reader's is the O6 gap itself. A per-KIND eighth stage is a scalar that can name ONE moment, so it cannot describe half this declaration. Wrong SHAPE for the fact, not the dearer branch. The third option died at the artifact too: a when inside the reader token string is refused by parse_refs as PROSE. THE BUMP WAS PROVED RATHER THAN ARGUED, and my first statement of it was the weaker kind. I originally rested it on lc-218's criterion that the version must identify the shape, which is a rule. The probe built to DISPROVE it settled it instead: against a floor-3 build a declaration carrying the object form returns declaration_malformed, with a bare-string control clean, so without the bump an older build reads a NEWER declaration and calls it BROKEN where schema_above_floor would refuse honestly. That is the floor guard's own stated failure, so the argument now rests on the mechanism. LAW 25 RUN IN FULL AND WIDER THAN LAST TIME: dry-run FIRST over every declared repo before any write, and every declared repo turned out to be TEN rather than the two lc-218 knew. Two applied clean; six blocked on content; two returned COULD NOT VERIFY for a LEDGER.md with no schema head at all. That split is tool-evidence for lc-239's not-mechanical premise rather than an opinion. SCOPE, NAMED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, which is the one thing lc-218 did not do: floor 4 covers THIS REPO and DOTFILES only. Dotfiles applied by the driving desk at d23529b and VERIFIED HERE at the artifact by my own read rather than from its report, declaration and all three carriers reading schema 4. The other EIGHT ride lc-239 BY ID, parked on the design round because 26 kinds need trigger values AUTHORED and the round is about to rule on that vocabulary. VERIFIED AT THIS DESK: suite 779 pass 0 fail 0 skip, --test 113 rows CLEAN, kind check CLEAN at 26 kinds, all four local carriers read schema 4. And independently of the lane's own tests, my own ten validation cases and a four-state exercise of read_moments, whose most important property is that NONE and UNDECLARED do not collapse: a declared absence of a moment and a missing declaration are different answers. WHAT THE LANE CONTRIBUTED BEYOND THE CODE: it caught its own red-first being non-discriminating mid-build, five assertions going red on old code for the wrong reason because a coarse shape-check already rejected the whole category, and rewrote them to assert message content before trusting the arrangement. Its baseline-green control did not exist until demanded and was produced then. Both are on lc-8's evidence in four-slot form. NOT DONE HERE AND NOT PRETENDED: the SURFACING half of O6, anything putting due reads in front of a session, is untouched and remains the gap the-loop.md ranks first; read_moments is reachable and tested but no verb calls it yet.

## lc-193
grade: DONE
requirement: THE BLOCKER EDGES ARE VALIDATED AND THE GRAPH IS NEVER TRAVERSED, SO A SET OF ITEMS CAN BE PERMANENTLY UNSCHEDULABLE WHILE EVERY CHECK READS CLEAN. Derived from the operator's player-loop frame, where the named failure is a SOFTLOCK — a state reached legitimately from which no progress is possible, and the game does not say so. check_blocker_targets resolves each id against both homes and reports a DANGLING one; the banner also reports a target that is DROPPED. Both are EDGE checks. Nothing asks whether the graph those edges form can ever drain. So lc-A blocked-by lc-B and lc-B blocked-by lc-A both resolve, neither is dangling, neither is dropped, and both items wait forever while item ready renders them as ordinary blocked work and item check reports CLEAN. The same holds for any chain terminating in a member that can never clear. THIS IS lc-14's 'PERMANENT SILENT PARK' REACHED BY A DIFFERENT ROUTE — that incident is quoted in check_blocker_targets' own docstring, and the check built from it watches the edge it came in on rather than the condition it names.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py
done-criterion: the blocker graph is TRAVERSED, and a set of items that can never become schedulable is a FINDING naming the members. OUTCOME FIRST: no item can wait forever while the board renders it as ordinary waiting. The computable slice is exactly two shapes and no more: a CYCLE among item-id blockers, and a CHAIN terminating in a member that can never clear. RED-FIRST: two items blocking each other must be named as a cycle; the control is that an ordinary chain which CAN drain (lc-179 behind lc-168) stays CLEAN, so the check does not fire on legitimate serialization — which is most of this carrier and is the way law 11 bites here. MUST-NOT-MOVE (1): a DECISION blocker is not a softlock and must never be reported as one — it is waiting on a party, which is the system working; only the graph's own unresolvability counts. MUST-NOT-MOVE (2): an EVIDENCE predicate that is simply not true yet is not a softlock either; lc-164 already grades those at booking and this check does not re-judge them. MUST-NOT-MOVE (3): it REPORTS, never unblocks — breaking a cycle is a judgment about which item was booked wrong.
evidence: DERIVED from the operator's player-loop frame, 2026-09-18, and it is the first mechanism that frame has produced rather than restated: the loop's named pathology is a softlock, and asking what a softlock is HERE gives a state the carrier can reach and cannot detect. MEASURED (this desk, with a positive control after a first search failed): grep for reachab|transitive|visited|graph|topolog|cycle across the package returns ZERO hits excluding the word lifecycle itself, while the same instrument finds 91 blocker-related lines in items.py — so the absence is a reading and not a dead pattern. NOTE ON THAT FIRST SEARCH, recorded because it is the day's own class committed while hunting it: the initial grep used 'cycle', which matches 'lifecycle' in every file, and returned a page of noise that read as coverage. DERIVED from reading items.py:1308-1470 at HEAD: the two existing checks resolve ids and report dangling or dropped targets — both are per-edge, and no traversal exists. RELAYED (opus review lane, same day): check_blocker_targets additionally DROPS an untypeable blocker entirely, booked as lc-186 — so the edge validation is incomplete as well as being the wrong altitude for this failure. NOT MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY NOT CLAIMED: no cycle was constructed and run against the real checks; the red-first arrangement is owed before this is believed, and the entry says so rather than resting on the reading.
blocked-by: NONE

MEASURED 2026-09-19 (this desk, executing each predicate through lanes.evaluate_trigger rather than reading it): the CHAIN-TERMINATING-IN-A-MEMBER-THAT-CAN-NEVER-CLEAR shape has a live test population in this carrier, at chain length ONE. ELEVEN live items carried an evidence blocker whose predicate is the literal string false, which exits 1 forever by construction; three of my regrades this session reduced it to EIGHT, which remain: lc-24, lc-53, lc-66, lc-82, lc-94, lc-136, lc-147, lc-199. THREE OF THE EIGHT ARE GRADED READY (lc-53, lc-94, lc-147), so a permanently unclearable wait currently sits under a schedulable grade. WHAT THE BOARD ACTUALLY RENDERS, stated precisely because the looser claim is what this entry must not rest on: item ready PRINTS the predicate text, so the literal false IS on screen and a reader who reads it can see the problem. The defect is the VERDICT WORD beside it, 'BLOCKED in the MACHINE's court ... Re-evaluated each pass', which asserts an automatic future resolution that cannot occur, on a wait that is in fact on another repo or on a person. A board is scanned by its verdict words, and all eight read as ordinary machine-waiting. WHY THE IDIOM EXISTS AND WHY THIS IS NOT EIGHT STRAGGLERS: classify_blocker (items.py:862-884) sanctions exactly three types, item / decision / evidence, and evidence demands an executable predicate. There is no type for 'blocked on an event this repo cannot test', which is what four of the eight actually are (lc-24, lc-53, lc-66, lc-147 all wait on claude-code-cache-fix). The false-with-a-comment idiom is authors reaching for the only spelling that parses. That gap is routed to the design round as its own finding; this entry carries the specimen, not the repair.
amend-reason: 2026-09-19 CORRECTION to the specimen added earlier today, landed in place rather than appended because a reader who stops at the first version takes the wrong account. The earlier text guessed that the false-predicate idiom was authors reaching for the only spelling that parses. It was not: commit 3352097 adopted it deliberately as a repair, and what it traded was a loud exit 127 for a silent exit 1. The guess was mine and unmeasured; the replacement is read off that commit's own message.

ELEVEN live items carried an evidence blocker whose predicate is the literal string false, which exits 1 forever by construction; three of my regrades this session reduced it to EIGHT, which remain: lc-24, lc-53, lc-66, lc-82, lc-94, lc-136, lc-147, lc-199. THREE OF THE EIGHT ARE GRADED READY (lc-53, lc-94, lc-147), so a permanently unclearable wait currently sits under a schedulable grade. WHAT THE BOARD ACTUALLY RENDERS, stated precisely because the looser claim is what this entry must not rest on: item ready PRINTS the predicate text, so the literal false IS on screen and a reader who reads it can see the problem. The defect is the VERDICT WORD beside it, 'BLOCKED in the MACHINE's court ... Re-evaluated each pass', which asserts an automatic future resolution that cannot occur, on a wait that is in fact on another repo or on a person. A board is scanned by its verdict words, and all eight read as ordinary machine-waiting. WHY THE IDIOM EXISTS AND WHY THIS IS NOT EIGHT STRAGGLERS: classify_blocker (items.py:862-884) sanctions exactly three types, item / decision / evidence, and evidence demands an executable predicate. There is no type for 'blocked on an event this repo cannot test', which is what four of the eight actually are (lc-24, lc-53, lc-66, lc-147 all wait on claude-code-cache-fix). THE IDIOM WAS NOT CARELESSNESS AND THE MEASURED ORIGIN IS SHARPER THAN THAT. It was adopted deliberately by a repair pass, commit 3352097 on 2026-09-13, whose own message states the case: eight of the carrier's ten evidence blockers had been written as PROSE, so the four that were READY reported FINDING [trigger_broken] exit 127 on every render, and the pass converted six of them to the false-with-a-comment form because that form was ALREADY IN THE CARRIER'S OWN DATA at lc-3 and lc-7. Those two are migration residue from BACKLOG.md, so a migration artifact became the sanctioned pattern by imitation, and the desk that generalised it was correctly reading the system's existing instances before changing it. The instance it found was itself the defect. WHAT THE PASS ACTUALLY TRADED, and this is the finding: exit 127 is BROKEN and LOUD, exit 1 is QUIET and silent forever, so the repair converted a visibly wrong answer into an invisibly wrong one. That is the exact inverse of this repo's own stated preference, that an over-firing check is annoying and visible while a falsely-passing one is lethal to learning. The commit's closing paragraph shows the desk reasoning about the third answer at the moment it chose quiet. So the repair is not to scold the idiom but to give the carrier a blocker type for a wait no local predicate can test; that gap is routed to the design round as its own finding, and this entry carries the specimen, not the repair.
closed-reason: 2026-09-19 2026-09-19 THE BLOCKER GRAPH IS TRAVERSED AND THE TRAVERSAL RUNS. Two acts: c060d47 built check_blocker_graph beside check_blocker_targets, 5eb8a7b wired it into cmd_item_check and proved its roster row. Both were needed and the second is the one that matters: before it the check existed, passed its tests, and NEVER RAN, so item check reported CLEAN over nine permanently-unschedulable items. That is lc-237's class exactly, a declared mechanism nothing evaluates, and shipping it would have minted a second instance in the same repo on the same day this desk booked the first. LIVE NOW, verified by running it: item check exits FINDING and names all nine. THE FIND THE BRIEF DID NOT PREDICT: I briefed an expected population of EIGHT, the literal-false items at chain length one. The traversal returns NINE, and the ninth is the item's whole justification: lc-67 is blocked by the live id lc-66, whose own effective blocker is the literal false, so lc-67 is exactly as unschedulable as lc-66 at chain length TWO. A per-EDGE check cannot see that by construction, which is what separates this item from check_blocker_targets. The lane investigated rather than tuning to my number, as instructed. ZERO CYCLES in the real carrier, reported as a measured zero rather than a silence: the cycle half is proven by a constructed ring in the roster row's own fire arm. NO LIVE DRAINABLE CHAIN EXISTS anywhere in this carrier, which is a finding in its own right: every live item-id blocker is stale or unclearable, so the control for ordinary serialization has no natural specimen and the lane reused the repo's existing BLOCKER_TARGET_LIVE_ITEMS fixture rather than minting a duplicate. LAW 11 HELD, three controls, one test each: a decision blocker is never reported as a softlock, an evidence predicate merely not-true-yet is never reported, and only the provably unclearable is. Most of this carrier is legitimate waiting and a check that could not tell the two apart would be worse than none. THE REACH LIMIT IS PRINTED rather than left in a docstring: without a declared id-prefix the traversal sees no edges, so no cycle and no multi-hop chain, while a length-one terminal still reports. The lane's choice of that narrower-but-always-checkable shape over a blanket could-not-verify is right and I ratified it, but its silence would have read as a clean graph verdict, which is a check reporting CLEAN over a population it never examined. THE ROW'S MUTATION COST TWO WRONG ANCHORS, recorded in 5eb8a7b because the failures are the instructive part: anchoring the chain half left the plant firing and prove-rows said so; anchoring the ring DECISION made the walk hang, which is worse than a wrong answer because it reports none. The recording line holds, proven 2/named to 0/unnamed with nothing else darkening. Coverage 93 to 94 of 114. The sharper half is that no parsing fixes an exclusive bucket set over a multi-valued field: ledger lines is genuinely both verb-written and session-written, and BOTH is a state the three buckets cannot express. My own naive count and the lane's parse_refs count agreed at 5/20/1 only because both resolve the mixed kind the same way, which is agreement on an axis neither varied rather than confirmation. amended-requirement: 2026-09-19 THE SESSION-START ANNOUNCEMENT CARRIES STATE AND NOT STRUCTURE, so a fact about what the repo IS gets re-derived by reasoning while it sits in a tracked file. AMENDED 2026-09-19 AT BUILD TIME, and the amendment corrects this entry's own design premise rather than its scope: the requirement said the split is verb-written VERSUS session-written, which assumes every kind has ONE writer. It does not. Kind ledger lines declares writer verb:ledger add, session, so it is BOTH, and an exclusive three-bucket split silently files it under verb. Read literally, the shipped line 20 of 26 writer:session therefore means session-ONLY, while 21 kinds actually have a session writer. That matters because the split exists to PREDICT SELF-ADMINISTRATION: a kind written by both a verb and a session is partly self-administering, and bucketing it as verb-written overstates the mechanized half by exactly the case a reader would most want to see. The readout must distinguish verb-only, session-only, BOTH, and other, or say plainly which of those its buckets mean. Same shape as lc-224's reader finding one field over: a per-kind scalar cannot describe a multi-valued fact, and the state the vocabulary cannot express renders as a neighbouring one that reads as ordinary.

THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS MOST, and it nearly shipped: the writer buckets are exclusive while the writer FIELD is not. ledger lines is verb:ledger add, session, so the original 20 of 26 writer:session invited the reading 20 kinds have a session writer when the honest one is 20 have ONLY a session writer and a twenty-first has one besides a verb. ## lc-175
grade: DONE
requirement: AN EVIDENCE BLOCKER'S DISCRIMINATION IS EXERCISED BY HAND AND THE CARRIER DOES NOT KNOW IT WAS. lc-164 made the BOOKING run a graded fact: exit 0 is refused, so every evidence blocker in a carrier answered 1 at its mint by construction. That proves the predicate can say NOT YET; it does not prove it can ever say ARRIVED, and one run cannot. The peer desk cachyos-setup-33 exercised three blockers by hand on 2026-09-18 — live exit, a constructed positive, a constructed negative — and NOTHING IN THE CARRIER RECORDS THAT THEY DID. An exercise nobody can see is a discipline that holds only while the person who held it is in the room; the slot is what makes its ABSENCE visible, which is the only thing that makes such a discipline survive a handoff.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/items.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/verbs.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/declaration.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/migrate.py,plugin/cli/lifecycle_core/refusals.py,test/test_items.py,test/test_schema.py
done-criterion: an evidence blocker carries the record of its exercise — the live exit at booking, and the two constructed arms that show the predicate answering BOTH ways — and a blocker without one is visible as such rather than silently unexercised. RED-FIRST: today the three blockers the peer desk exercised are indistinguishable in the carrier from three nobody touched. MUST-NOT-MOVE: the constructed arms are the AUTHOR'S act, never the tool's — a verb that synthesised a positive would be grading its own plant, which is the same-parentage defect law 2 exists against; and the slot must not make every existing blocker a finding overnight (UNKNOWN is the migration's declared transitional value).
evidence: lc-164, closed 2026-09-18 at 873023b, which shipped the booking-run grade and named this as the half it does NOT ship. The peer desk's own reasoning, relayed as theirs: running a predicate is computable and its exit code is a fact rather than a judgment, which clears the mechanism bar — but the SECOND and THIRD arms are constructed by the author, so this slot is a form whose absence is readable, not a predicate the tool can compute. A new LIVE slot is a schema change: law 25 binds it to one migration, dry-run over every declared repo.

amend-reason: 2026-09-19 2026-09-19 pickup re-grounding, the design's own mechanism 5, run before any line of this item was written. Two supersessions. The entry's stated carrier wave (lc-168) closed without it, which is the stale-premise class and is exactly what a pickup re-read exists to catch: nobody holds both halves at once, since the wave that closed is recorded elsewhere than the entry that expected it. And the entry's schema claim, which it asserted as a rule, is now an executed result with a control, so the bump rests on the mechanism rather than on inherited reasoning. Recorded on both bundle entries identically because both make the same claim and both fail the same premise.
amended-evidence: 2026-09-19 lc-164, closed 2026-09-18 at 873023b, which shipped the booking-run grade and named this as the half it does NOT ship. The peer desk's own reasoning, relayed as theirs: running a predicate is computable and its exit code is a fact rather than a judgment, which clears the mechanism bar — but the SECOND and THIRD arms are constructed by the author, so this slot is a form whose absence is readable, not a predicate the tool can compute. A new LIVE slot is a schema change: law 25 binds it to one migration, dry-run over every declared repo. PICKUP RE-GROUNDING 2026-09-19, executing desk, wave B. TWO FINDINGS, one killing a premise and one proving a claim this entry only asserted. FIRST, THE BOOKED PREMISE IS DEAD: this entry says lc-168 is the wave that carries its new slot. lc-168 CLOSED on 2026-09-18 carrying the SEVENTH KIND STAGE, not this item slot, and MEASURED here at pickup: it sits in ITEMS-DONE.md graded DONE. So the migration this entry expected to ride has already closed without it, and the slot now needs its own act. The blocker reads resolved for the same reason, so nothing is waiting. SECOND, THE SCHEMA CLAIM IS NOW PROVED BY EXECUTION rather than asserted: this entry states that a new LIVE slot is a schema change, which was inherited reasoning. PROBE RUN at this desk against TODAY's build, with a control: an ITEMS.md entry carrying an extra slot returns FINDING [item_shape] block carries unknown slot(s), exit 2, while the byte-identical entry without it returns exit 0 clean. So the item slot vocabulary is CLOSED, and a carrier written under the new code is read by an older build as MALFORMED rather than refused honestly through schema_above_floor. That is the same mechanism proved for lc-224 act 2 one field over, and it is the bump's real argument rather than a restatement of lc-218's criterion. CONSEQUENCE, carried not buried: this owes its OWN numbered bump under lc-218's MUST-NOT-BUILD (one number, one command per bump, so a failure names one cause), law 25 binds it to a dry-run over EVERY declared repo, and every declared repo is TEN rather than the two lc-218 knew. THE SHAPE WAS DECIDED BY MEASUREMENT, not symmetry: 8 evidence blockers / 3 READY and 25 decision blockers / 16 READY on this carrier, so a member of the fixed SLOTS run carrying the migration's UNKNOWN would have made 3 items ready_with_unknown_slot findings the day it shipped - which this entry's MUST-NOT-MOVE forbids. Hence a CONDITIONAL slot on the DONE_ONLY_SLOTS precedent, absent from UNKNOWNABLE_SLOTS, its absence counted (I5's move from the-loop.md: a count makes a zero answerable where a better duty does not). TWO AUTHORS IN ONE RECORD: the live exit is handed back from lc-164's existing booking run, never re-executed (that item's own MUST-NOT-MOVE) and never hardcoded as 1, which would assert an observation nobody made if the grading moved; the two constructed arms are the AUTHOR'S, because a verb synthesising a positive would grade its own plant. grade: DONE
requirement: THE EROSION PROBE IS DESIGNED AND NOBODY IS BOOKED TO RUN IT. lc-222 closed on its designed-probe branch and produced the design; nothing books EXECUTING it. That is this arc's own assumed-delivery class, a step with no named actor does not fail but ACCUMULATES, and it matters rather than being tidy because directive addendum 1 makes the probe's RESULT the thing that bounds further always-on mechanism admission. A design alone bounds nothing. Found at the driving desk 2026-09-19 by searching the carrier for the run and getting zero hits. Record: lc-222's closure, commit 9d97840.
goal: enforce-the-invariants
write-set: docs/audits/
done-criterion: The longitudinal survival figure EXISTS as a results artifact under docs/audits/: of the roster rows PROVEN at each sampled earlier commit, the fraction still proving at HEAD, per row. BOTH RIDERS HONOURED OR THE RESULT DOES NOT COUNT. (1) A previously-proven row that stops proving because its mutation ANCHOR moved under a legitimate refactor is COULD NOT VERIFY, reported apart and never counted as erosion; without the split, ordinary refactor churn manufactures the finding. (2) The result STATES ITS REACH: the roster channel only, silent on rule-corpus effects on session behaviour. VERDICT PRE-REGISTERED per lc-222's MUST-NOT-BUILD: regression among previously-proven rows rising with roster size is erosion; flat survival under a growing roster is health; dilution by new rows is neither and must not move the figure. A run whose outcome is consistent with both does not close this.

DATA EXISTS: git holds every past roster state. TWO OPERATING CONSTRAINTS, BOTH MEASURED AT THIS DESK 2026-09-19 rather than anticipated. prove-rows REFUSES TO START on a dirty tree, its stated reason being provenance; I hit that refusal live. And the method is already exercised: I ran `prove-rows capture_dominated` inside a detached worktree at 5172068 to establish that a crash predated a change, which is this probe's own technique proven on one row before anyone rests on it. KNOWN POPULATION NOTE: at least one row (capture_dominated, lc-197) is unproven for an arrangement reason and must land in the could-not-verify column, not the erosion one.
